And on the side of the Honourable Stanisław Chełmski, heir of the estates of Łukomia with appurtenances, against the Honourable Antoni Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, heir of the estates of Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały or Nowa Wieś and others, and against the Most Reverend Konstanty Jełowiecki, Abbot, and his Ląd Convent, heirs of the estates of Drzewce, [and against] Ignacy Gałecki, Starost of Bydgoszcz, heir of the estates of Biskupice and the deserted lands of Bystrzyce — likewise issued in the Konin municipal court on Wednesday after the Feast of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary, that is on 16 August of the same year —

[the commission] orders all these parties to appear and file their court appearances.

[Translator's note: Party-initiated citations: The citations were not issued by the commission on its own motion but were initiated by the parties themselves, each going to the relevant municipal court to have the instruments entered in the records. Prusimski filed five citations covering a wide range of collateral neighbours and co-heirs; Chełmski filed one, naming Prusimski, the Ląd Convent (Jełowiecki), and Gałecki. The commission's role was to act on those citations by ordering the named parties to appear. This was standard early modern Polish procedure: a party initiated the citation, the court recorded and issued it, and the commission enforced appearance on that basis.]

And since the Honourable Antoni Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, heir of the estates of Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś, Osiny, Łazy, Szetlew and the olędry of Osiny, appeared personally, as plaintiff and defendant; the Honourables Walenty Wardęski, Stanisław Jaroszewski, Wojciech Wardęski, Sebastian Krzyżkowski, Kasper Kurowski, co-heirs and possessors of their portions of the village of Kurowo Wielki, appeared personally as defendants; the Honourable Maci Jaroszewski, son of the late Honourable Walenty, Jan Jaroszewski, son of the late Honourable Stanisław Jaroszewski, and likewise Maci Jaroszewski, Jan's own nephew and son of the late Paweł, and also the Honourable Urszula née Sławińska, widow of the late Honourable Piotr Fulwarski, in her own name and in the name of her children, co-heirs and possessors of their portions of the manorial village of Szetlew, appeared personally as defendants; the Honourable Józef Pawłowski, summoned personally for the giving of testimony, appeared — all of whom the court of the dispute having heard, and since not only the Crown law requires that collateral neighbours on the boundary be summoned, but the constitution or commission enacted by the Estates likewise recommends the same:

this court therefore orders the Honourable Prusimski to submit and produce in court, in accordance with the enacted constitution, the collateral citations of his corner neighbours and collateral parties. The Honourable Prusimski, complying with the orders of his court, submitted the aforementioned citations, issued both against the principal parties bordering his estates and against the collateral parties; and upon their production and compliance with

the sentence of his court, the court declares him free in this respect, and orders the parties to proceed further with their pleadings. In the further pleading of the parties, the court of the dispute hearing them and proceeding in accordance with the terms of the constitution and the appointed dates, finds that for more thorough information and the showing of the boundaries and the corner mound, it is necessary to submit all documents concerning the boundaries and corner mounds of the aforementioned estates, and likewise the sworn inquiries conducted both in respect of the corner mounds at the past field inspections and in respect of the transgressions committed in former times and of the claimed stawisko, and particularly in the year 1728 — as also specified in the Tribunal decrees and the present commission for submission — and orders the Honourable Prusimski to submit, without delay and under oath in his court, all documents relating to the present boundary cause, together with the sworn inquiries mentioned above, and likewise the collateral neighbours [to submit theirs].

The parties complying with the orders of their court: the Honourable Prusimski submitted both the paired sworn inquiries in sealed rolls and the documents listed in a schedule signed by his court [representative], and also others beyond the schedule contained in bundles, and offers to swear to them. The Honourable Maci Jaroszewski likewise submitted a copy of the boundaries between the estates of Szetlew and Szetlewek of the year 1581. The Honourable Urszula Fulwarska likewise submitted a copy of the partition among the Trąbczyński family of the estates of Trąbczyn, Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś, Osiny, Łazy and others, effected in 1693, to be found in the Pyzdry municipal court — which [documents] the Most Honourable and Honourable President signed at the present commission — and they offer to swear that they have no more. The other co-heirs of the estates of Szetlew and Kurowo declare under oath that they have no documents relating to the present boundary cause nor can they submit any. The parties present in their court appearance, and in particular the heir of Trąbczyn, not content with this production, demand from one another a fuller production before the oath is sworn.

This court therefore, having suspended the oath over the submitted documents, orders the parties named above appearing in the court appearance to produce their documents more fully. And since the parties, instead of a fuller production, declare under oath that they have no further documents nor can they produce them, and prepare to swear honestly to the documents [already submitted], this court, resolving the suspended oath, orders that these oaths be performed, with this formula:

and indeed by the Honourable Antoni Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, Maci Jaroszewski, and Urszula Fulwarska, with the following formula: that they have produced honestly and justly all documents relating to the boundary cause of their estates and have not concealed any to the detriment and harm of their neighbour. And the Honourables and the Honest Walenty Wardęski, Stanisław Jaroszewski, Wojciech Wardęski, Sebastian Krzyżkowski, Kasper Kurowski, co-heirs of Kurowo Wielki: that they have no documents relating to the present boundary cause of those estates, nor do they know of any, nor do they conceal any. To the performance of this oath and the announcement of its formula the Court Summoner was assigned. All performed the oath with the formula expressed above. After the performance of this oath, the court, having released the parties from further production of documents, orders the same parties to continue with their pleadings — without regard to the non-appearance of the Honourable Chełmski, his brother, and others — by virtue of the constitution. In the further pleading of the parties, having heard their dispute, and having suspended for the time being the unsealing and reading of the sworn inquiries submitted to its court — the better to take note of and to establish the places and names mentioned in the sworn inquiries and documents, and the better to identify the corner mounds formed differently by each party, and finally to establish whether supplementary sworn inquiries will be needed — the court orders the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, heir of the estates of Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały or Nowa Wieś, Osiny, Łazy, and Szetlewek, to show and walk out his boundary line between his estates of Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś and others, and the inherited estates of Łukomia with appurtenances of the Honourable Chełmski, for execution — to the execution of which the court offers to proceed.

[Section 4: The Boundary Line — Prusimski's Case]

The Honourable Prusimski, complying with the orders of his court, having proceeded to the execution of his boundary line and establishing his starting point, began that boundary line between these estates and Łukomia

and the other inherited estates of the Honourable Chełmski, from the place and boundary mounds described in the [commission] act, where having set his court, asserting that here, here in this place and nowhere else, is the confluence of the grounds of three estates, namely Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś. Confirming this confluence, he showed first the Łukomia pine forest and woodland, lying to the west, to the right beyond the beaten road running from Zagórów and Drzewce, belonging and used without any dispute by Łukomia; his own Trąbczyn woodland, lying to the south on the other side of that road, called Ostrowce in this place, belonging to Trąbczyn from of old and in current use, but contested by Łukom; and the Drzewce pine forest lying to the north, not in dispute, used by Drzewce up to this point now and formerly — thereby demonstrating the evident confluence, that is the convergence of these three estates. To further confirm this convergence he showed two boundary mounds: one well-marked and evident; the second not so very marked but having the roundness and likeness of a mound and a small ditch around it, that is a crown; and a third low mound, not very marked, which he asserted to be a damaged one — lying a few paces from one another, in a triangle, in this place between the roads described in the act, to be found there and recorded in the past field acts.

And further, testing the position, that is the position of these corner mounds, as expressed and described in the judicial site inspection of 1592, known from the road that leads to Pyzdry between the estates of Trąbczyn Mały, Osiny, Łukomia, and the properties of the Ląd Convent — he showed first of all the old road, sufficiently still marked and deep, overgrown, lying a good half-staj before the mounds, described in the present act, asserting that this is that road, not the road from Osiny, and that it is the ancient Pyzdry road, properly running between the estates of Trąbczyn Mały, Osiny, Łukomia, and the properties of the Ląd Convent, that is Drzewce, Zagórów, and Wrąbczyn, all the way to Pyzdry itself; whereas the road from Osiny now running to Pyzdry via Łukom came into use only when that other road was blocked up and abandoned, that is, changed from the Osiny road to the Pyzdry road — but nevertheless this Osiny road too branches off from that same old Pyzdry road above [i.e., to the north] before these corner mounds, and then rejoins the same road beyond Łukomia — which he also showed to the court.

And further, testing also what the people say — that these corner mounds should be and lie beside the road from Drzewce going towards Łukomia — the Honourable Prusimski says that that same Zagórów road passing through Drzewce is at the same time the Drzewce road, because taking it from the closer village of Drzewce it can be called nothing but the Drzewce road; and he immediately shows also another old road, still sufficiently marked, branching off to the right from the second, the Zagórów road and at the same time the Drzewce road, emerging close to the corner mounds beneath the hill alongside which runs the road from Osiny to Pyzdry by a shortcut, joining the present Pyzdry road and running together with the Osiny road towards Pyzdry and towards Łukom — showing precisely the position of the corner mounds, as the people too say.

And further, testing the assertion that this third mound was scattered by the former heirs of Łukomia, he submits a manifest of 1719, on Tuesday after the Sunday called Reminiscere, in the Konin municipal court, concerning the scattering of the corner mound dividing the estate of Trąbczyn, Łomowo belonging to the principal village of Łukomia, and Drzewce; and likewise the judicial site inspection of 1726, on Tuesday after Saint Francis the Confessor, conducted officially in the Pyzdry municipal court concerning the dispersal and demolition of the Trąbczyn mound; and further the manifest entered under the act of that same judicial site inspection, concerning a similar dispersal and ploughing up of the mounds by the late Honourable Krzysztof Prusimski, his grandfather, heir of Trąbczyn with appurtenances, against the former heirs of Łukomia, predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski — and moreover he additionally submits a manifest and invokes a like proceeding, that is concerning the damage to one mound and the private removal of another in the Grądzeń boundary with the Ląd Convent, lodged in the Konin municipal court on Friday after Low Sunday of 1733 against the predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski, heirs of Łukomia — and this same mound the Honourable Prusimski now protesting declares to have been actual formerly and thereafter damaged.

[Translator's note: Prywatnie usucie: asterisked in the source, indicating an uncertain reading in the transcription. The phrase most likely means the private removal or pulling-out of a mound. Translated accordingly.]

And further, proving that the Honourable Chełmski himself acknowledged these corner mounds at the past three field sessions, and moreover together with his brother acknowledged them as corner mounds — with only the difference that he held and acknowledged them to be not between Trąbczyn, Drzewce, and Łukomia as

the Convent and the Honourable Prusimski maintained and acknowledged, but between Łukomia, Bukowe, and Drzewce — he submits three field decrees: the first of 1759, adjudicated in the field between these parties by two Sieradz Boundary Surveyors, filed in the Pyzdry municipal court on Thursday after the Sunday called Cantate in the same year; the second of 1760, made in the field on the Monday after the Sunday called Exaudi by the Łęczyca Boundary Surveyor in the Drzewce boundary with Trąbczyn, filed in the Konin municipal court on Friday on the very feast of Saint Anthony of Padua of the same year; the third established by the Łęczyca and Gostyń Boundary Surveyors on the Monday after Saint Bartholomew of 1760, filed in the Konin municipal court on the Monday after the Sunday called Jubilate of 1761.

And to further prove that here is the true corner mound and boundary mounds — the better to verify them — he showed thirty visible mounds of the same form and size as the first prominent one in the corner mound, lying in the boundary between Drzewce and Trąbczyn without great difficulty of identification, and described from these corner mounds to the second corner mound and confluence of the villages of Skokum, Drzewce Convent, and Trąbczyn inherited by the Honourable Prusimski — set up by the field decree of 1760 by the Łęczyca Boundary Surveyor, expressed above from the Oblata.

In this same showing, the Honourable Prusimski also remonstrates to his court that the Honourable Chełmski — forming for himself both at the past three field sessions and now a boundary as though between Drzewce and Łukomia, and as though from that corner mound consistently acknowledged at all times during the three field sessions by the Convent, which the serfs from Drzewce present here below named now also acknowledge — stretching to a second supposed corner mound at the head, that is at the beginning, of the woodland called Ostrowce, near the Osiny road itself, established by him, through fourteen mounds only:

not only takes for himself and appropriates, from the aforementioned thirty boundary mounds of Drzewce and Trąbczyn, fourteen for his invented boundary; but also, converting one of these fourteen mounds and then again a fifteenth Drzewce boundary mound — although lying progressively in one line at a distance from one another — into corner mounds, and taking a third overturned stump still further from them as a corner mound, forms and establishes for himself from such mounds a corner mound at the head of the Ostrowce woodland near the Osiny road itself, as though lying and existing between Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn; and by this corner mound takes from Trąbczyn the entire Ostrowce woodland, as the heirs and people of Trąbczyn maintain it is called and has always belonged to Trąbczyn.

This woodland, together with the pine forest, beginning from there, that is from the Honourable Chełmski's corner mound and reaching as far as the Honourable Prusimski's corner mound and to the road running from Zagórów and Drzewce to Kalisz, Chocz, and Tomice, the people assert has belonged to Trąbczyn from of old. And as it is properly Trąbczyn's, the Honourable Prusimski submits in support the judicial site inspection in the Pyzdry municipal court of 1644, on Monday after the Sunday called Rogationum, conducted on behalf of the late Honourables Marcin, Wojciech, Władysław, and Andrzej Trąbczyński, heirs of Trąbczyn, in the Ostrowce woodland belonging to Trąbczyn, concerning eight fresh blazes made on pine trees and one hundred and fifty trees of various kinds cut during hunting for stakes for nets, against the late Honourable Jerzy Chełmski, heir of Łukomia.

And likewise the proceedings relating to that judicial site inspection, that is two default decrees: the first concerning the hunting and killing of game in the Trąbczyn woodland; the second concerning the cutting of timber for stakes for nets to the number of one hundred and fifty pieces, and likewise for other purposes to the number of one hundred pieces, also in the Trąbczyn woodland — obtained on behalf of the above-named Honourables Trąbczyński and Anna née Słończyc, widow of the late Stanisław Trąbczyński, against the late Honourable Jerzy Chełmski, heir of Łukomia, in the Konin land court in 1644 on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi, under one act.

And likewise two Konin land court decrees in the same year between these parties, on Monday after Saint Hedwig, with motions to the Tribunal admitted, and two Piotrków Tribunal decrees of deferral in this cause on both counts against the same Honourable Chełmski, issued in 1645 on Tuesday before the Feast of Saint Prisca from the aforementioned motions, and made.

And moreover two further judicial site inspections in respect of that same Ostrowce woodland, with its name expressly mentioned and its belonging to Trąbczyn stated among other woodlands: the first of 1724, on Wednesday after Saint Prisca, in the Konin municipal court; and the second in the Pyzdry municipal court after Saint Catherine of 1725 — both conducted on behalf of and at the suit of the late Honourable Krzysztof Prusimski, heir of Trąbczyn and grandfather of the Honourable Starost of Niszczewice, against the late Honourable Józef Chełmski, heir of Łukomia — and likewise the proceedings conducted in this matter, expressed in the schedule, he submits.

And finally, proving that Bukowe — whether deserted lands or merely a name for ground lying within the Łukomia boundaries — has no convergence of any kind and no separate boundaries, and cannot have a corner mound here in the place where the Honourable [Prusimski] now and formerly, and likewise the Ląd Convent together with him, established their corner mound — and that here the convergence and corner mound of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn as shown by the Honourable Prusimski is and must be the actual one — and likewise proving the former demarcation of Trąbczyn with Drzewce, he submits not only the above-mentioned field decree of 1760, made from sworn inquiries and documents expressed above from the Oblata, which sets out Bukowe as ground lying only within the Łukomia boundaries having no convergence nor separate boundaries, not touching this place here, and situated and found near Imielno — but also the documents expressed below relating to this circumstance and to this corner mound.

And firstly from those, as to the Drzewce boundary with Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś, he submits a deed in the prescribed order for a compromise between the heir of Trąbczyn and the Ląd Convent concerning the demarcation of the estates of Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały with the Convent villages, of 1,443, on Monday the day after the Sunday called Reminiscere, attested in the Konin land court records and filed in the Poznań municipal court on Friday after Saint Michael of 1761 —

asserting that according to this compromise, or some other act which cannot be found on account of the lapse of time, the mounds in the Drzewce and Trąbczyn boundary, set up from the Łukomia corner mound to this corner mound here, still sufficiently marked, of which there are thirty, must have been heaped.

[Translator's note: Ob revolutionem temporum: literally "on account of the revolution of the times", a standard Latin legal formula meaning that the document in question has been lost or cannot be produced due to the passage of time.]

And in the later period, as to the second, the Łukomia boundary with Trąbczyn, he submits two default decrees:

the first concerning the Łukomia boundary, obtained against the late Honourable Jerzy Chełmski and others, co-heirs of the jointly held villages or estates of Łukomia, Imielno, and Łomowo — as directly expressed in this default — concerning the demarcation of the said estates of Łukomia, Imielno, and Łomowo, lying in one ground, from Trąbczyn and other villages, the principal defendants;

the second against the Ląd Convent, heirs of the village of Drzewce, summoned collaterally likewise concerning the demarcation of Drzewce from Trąbczyn, for the same appointed date, at the suit and action of the heirs of Trąbczyn with appurtenances, namely the aforementioned Trąbczyński family and Dybryński —

Both obtained in the Konin land court in 1644 on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi, under one act, without the slightest mention or reference to Bukowe.

And likewise two decrees of motions, similarly concerning the demarcation of those estates between the same parties — and indeed with the heirs of Łukomia, Imielno, and Łomowo, with the same expression that Łukom, Imielno, and Łomowo lie in one ground — likewise without any mention whatsoever of Bukowe, issued in the same Konin land court in the same year on Monday after Saint Hedwig, after the above-mentioned default.

And moreover, to confirm his corner mound, the Honourable Prusimski submits the compromise decree between the present Honourable Chełmski, heir of Łukomia with other appurtenances, and the Ląd Convent, of 1761, on Thursday after the Sunday called Misericordiae, made in the field, after the above-mentioned field decree of 1760 —

acknowledging no convergence nor any Bukowe boundary, but only setting out the ground called Bukowe as lying within the Łukomia boundaries, and showing moreover that neither Imielno nor Bukowe are bounded off from Łukomia of itself, but lie within the body of the Łukomia boundaries — filed in the Kalisz municipal court on the Monday after the Sunday Cantate.

By this decree, though made without the court appearance of the Honourable Prusimski, the Honourable Prusimski tests and proves that the Honourable Chełmski, in running out his boundary with Drzewce, made no mention either of a corner mound or of the boundary itself, and moreover began his Łukomia — not Bukowe — boundary with Drzewce from the Zagórów corner mound, and ran and established it between Łukomia and Drzewce — not between Bukowe — and determined it no further than a staj before the Honourable Prusimski's corner mound, directing it straight into that corner mound, and allowed the compromise court to mark it with mounds only up to that point. And by this his own boundary he confirmed and acknowledged not only the Łukomia — not Bukowe — boundary with Drzewce, but also the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski and the convergence of Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś. For these two boundaries of Łukomia with Drzewce, having no other third estate before them — especially being determined so close before the corner mound — can only reach Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś as their third, and must necessarily share the same convergence and the same corner mound together, and must necessarily end and be determined thus and in no other way at the corner mound formed by the Honourable Prusimski, and likewise by the Ląd Convent, during the three field sessions and now shown.

It follows therefore that Bukowe cannot be in this place and at the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski and the Ląd Convent, and cannot have any convergence of its own there; nor is there or can there be a corner mound of Bukowe, Łukomia, and Drzewce in this place, as the Honourable Chełmski alleged at the three sessions

and now alleges — but the true and genuine corner mound of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś is here, as the Honourable Prusimski and the Convent established it at the three field sessions, and as the Honourable Starost now establishes and shows it. And since therefore the true corner mound of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn is here, and the Honourable Chełmski vacillates in his allegation of the corner mound — both his corner mounds, the one which he places at the starting point at the head of the Ostrowce woodland right by the Osiny road, also against the judicial site inspection of 1592, and the one which he places at the terminal point as though by reason of the convergence of Bukowe at the corner mound of the Honourable Starost, putting Bukowe in place of Trąbczyn and pushing Trąbczyn aside — and likewise the entire boundary of the Honourable Chełmski through thirteen Drzewce and Trąbczyn boundary mounds as though forming a Łukomia boundary with Drzewce between these two corner mounds — are wrongly and unlawfully invented.

The Honourable Prusimski says and proves that his own corner mound is good and lawful. And further, still asserting this corner mound and calling to his court's memory the three above-mentioned field decrees, the Honourable Prusimski says: although the Convent is not now present in writing here at this act, and does not appear itself but only the Drzewce serfs —

nevertheless this same corner mound and confluence of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn — not Bukowe — during the three field sessions held

with the Honourable Chełmski, it unfailingly acknowledged in this place together with him, and moreover the Convent itself, as to the Drzewce boundary with Łukomia, established and ran it from the Zagórów corner mound together with the Honourable Chełmski all the way to here, and proved this with a sworn inquiry — which the same Convent's serfs from Drzewce and from Trąbczyn, named above in the boundary line account, now acknowledge and maintain. And moreover the Convent, not acknowledging either of the corner mounds nor the boundary of the Honourable Chełmski, and never acknowledging any convergence of Bukowe either — for all of this protested always together with the Honourable Prusimski, as their protests recorded in the field decrees attest, against the Honourable Chełmski — consistently maintained and acknowledged this corner mound. Which corner mound also now, together with the roads, the people from Drzewce present here, in the absence of their lords —

as likewise from Trąbczyn, ready to acknowledge it when questioned by their court — namely from Drzewce: the labourers Tomasz and Kazimierz Czerniakowic, Tomasz and Fabian, Stefan Głąbik the juror, Jan Kuwal, Kazimierz Dąbek the wheelwright; from Trąbczyn: Szyman Boderek aged ninety-nine, Bartłomiej the Court Summoner, Maci Wąchnicki the forester, Piotr, Szyman, [uncertain: narożnik], Wojciech Zelak — and likewise the Honourable Józef Pawłowski, a nobleman raised here in the neighbourhood and serving with the predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski — showing the pine forests and woodlands of each estate separately, namely: the Łukomia pine forest lying to the west, to the right of the Zagórów road and at the same time the Drzewce road, belonging to Łukomia from of old and thus long called and named; and the Ostrowce woodland with pine forest

so called, the Trąbczyn [woodland], lying to the south on the other side of that same road, belonging to Trąbczyn from of old and in current use, now contested, touching the corner mounds; and the Drzewce pine forest lying to the north, touching the Ostrowce woodland, marked by mounds still beyond the Osiny road — taken by the Honourable Chełmski into his boundary — belonging to and used by Drzewce from of old up to those mounds: acknowledging not only the confluence but also the corner mound together with the three mounds of these three estates of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś — keeping their corner mound albeit in the absence of their lords — and not acknowledging any corner mound or boundary of Bukowe. And likewise the old road shown by the Honourable Prusimski lying several dozen paces before the corner mounds, being well marked and beaten, they acknowledge as the old Pyzdry road, and say and acknowledge that from Osiny, Trąbczyn, and other villages they have gone past Łukomia of the Honourable Chełmski, Drzewce, Zagórów, and Wrąbczyn — the Convent villages — to Pyzdry from of old.

And the Osiny road, now turned towards Pyzdry via Łukom not so very long ago — and that this Osiny road branches off further above [i.e., to the north] from that same old Pyzdry road before the corner mounds towards Osiny, and afterwards rejoins the same road again beyond Łukomia — and likewise

the Zagórów road when one goes from Drzewce — and the Drzewce road branching from it — they name and acknowledge. And likewise they acknowledge the Ostrowce woodland: that it is here in this place, and not another, and has been called thus from of old.

The Honourable Prusimski having thus submitted these proofs at the starting point and demonstrated the truth and substance of his [corner mound], having proved this here at once, first protests against the Honourable Chełmski, heir of Łukomia with appurtenances: that although he well knows that in this place there is the true confluence of the grounds and pine forests of three estates, namely Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś, and that from this very place these estates have used and encompassed the woodland and pine forests, and that the Ostrowce woodland with pine forest belonging to Trąbczyn from of old and in current use reaches to this place, and that accordingly in this place the true corner mound is here and the three mounds are truly corner mounds — which he himself acknowledged at the three field sessions, with only the difference that he judged Bukowe in place of Trąbczyn — whereas the heirs of Drzewce and Trąbczyn, pushing back the estate of Bukowe as having no separate boundaries but lying within the boundaries of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn, acknowledged it —

and finally he himself acknowledged and confirmed the same convergence and his own boundary, run from the Zagórów corner mound between Łukomia and Drzewce — not between Bukowe — during the compromise with the Ląd Convent, and determined to stop only a staj before this corner mound, directed straight towards it — and thereby not only the convergence and his alleged corner mound both

as placed at the starting point at the head of the Ostrowce woodland and at the terminal point, but also his own boundary through thirteen mounds as though formed between Drzewce and Łukomia by himself — he utterly destroyed.

And likewise that he wrongly and unlawfully forms and establishes for himself both these corner mounds — both at the starting point and terminal point — and between them as though a Łukomia boundary with Drzewce; and moreover that even according to the judicial site inspection of 1592, which the Honourable Chełmski takes as his greatest foundation for the present cause, there is and is to be found the old Pyzdry road, still sufficiently marked, beaten and deep, lying a few lasek above towards Drzewce before these mounds, which the people also remember and as expressed above have acknowledged — and that this same road, as the judicial site inspection records, runs between the estates of Trąbczyn Mały, Osiny, Łukomia, and the properties of the Ląd Convent, that is Drzewce, Zagórów, and Wrąbczyn belonging to Ląd, all the way to Pyzdry; and that from this old road the present road by which one now travels from Osiny, Trąbczyn Mały and other estates to Pyzdry branches off not far before these mounds towards Osiny, and afterwards rejoins that same old road again beyond Łukomia, and from that junction continues all the way to Pyzdry itself —

nevertheless, notwithstanding all this, to the diminishment and harm of the grounds and pine forests of the heir of the estates of Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś, the said Honourable Chełmski, acting wrongly and unjustly, at the head, that is at the beginning, of the said Ostrowce woodland belonging to Trąbczyn from of old — as the judicial site inspection of 1644, on Monday after the Sunday of the Cross, conducted by Wojciech, Władysław, and Andrzej Trąbczyński, heirs of Trąbczyn, and another later one by Krzysztof Prusimski, heirs of Trąbczyn, against the heirs of Łukomia, and the proceedings therein, attest —

establishes and forms for himself a corner mound on the proper ground of Trąbczyn Mały, and at the same time another as though for Bukowe, Łukomia, and Drzewce, in the place of the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski and the Ląd Convent, taking two Drzewce and Trąbczyn boundary mounds and a third overturned stump of wood — not in a triangle but lying far from one another; and in his boundary from the supposed Bukowe corner mound to his second corner mound at the head of the Ostrowce woodland he takes and turns thirteen Drzewce and Trąbczyn boundary mounds; and thereby wrongly intends to appropriate and is appropriating the ground and Ostrowce woodland, and further the marshes or stawisko, and likewise the woodlands Miąsykąt, Dziadulina and others — all proper Trąbczyn property.

Then secondly against the Ląd Convent, and more particularly against the Reverend Father Sojecki their plenipotentiary: that the said Convent, and more particularly their plenipotentiary, notwithstanding that they always at the past several field sessions consistently acknowledged the corner mound of Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn — not Bukowe — in this same place where the Honourable Prusimski now establishes it, or rather they themselves established it as to the Drzewce boundary with Łukomia from the Zagórów corner mound together with the Honourable Chełmski all the way to here, and proved it by sworn inquiry — which the same Convent's serfs from Drzewce, and from Trąbczyn named above in the boundary line account, now acknowledge and maintain —

nevertheless now making some pretence of doubt regarding their acknowledgement, and thinking as one with the Honourable Chełmski, they are now joining him, and having taken a lead from the Honourable Chełmski against the protesting party, and not blocking the corner mound to the diminishment of the ground of the complainant — which he established at the head of the Ostrowce woodland, and which they never acknowledged before and against which they protested, and indeed contrary to their own acknowledgement and recognition

as clergy, and contrary to all justice and the proofs — acting in the matter of the ruin and wrong of the complainant with the Honourable Chełmski from the same platform, and wrongly revoking their recognition as though made through ignorance of the documents which they had then and now — to the scandal of secular persons — acknowledge that corner mound to the Honourable Chełmski, do not contest or block it, and thereby both the said Convent and the Honourable Chełmski intend and are preparing to appropriate the complainant's proper ground and to deprive him of it to his detriment.

And therefore by this their acknowledgement and recognition contrary to their former position they are giving cause — as it were — for greater dispute and costs to the parties (which as ecclesiastics they ought above all to avoid, and are doing so perhaps with the purpose, as noted above, of the agreement during the compromise made between them concerning the boundaries, and now evaded upon submission — concerning the invalidity of which insofar as it might prejudice him, the Honourable Prusimski makes a formal protest); and they are acting and proceeding contrary to their own acknowledgement and recognition already confirmed by decrees, and thereby draw upon themselves the penalties prescribed by law, and will be obliged to be liable to them and likewise to return costs wrongfully caused to the complainant.

[The Boundary Line Continued — Mounds, Stawisko, and Cygany]

The Honourable Prusimski, having shown the corner mound and submitted proofs and lodged his protests, proceeding further with his boundary line and the boundary between Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś and Łukomia and running it toward the south — he went from the said corner mounds a little by a shortcut to the right, between south and west, and having crossed the road from Zagórów and Drzewce running towards Kalisz, Chocz, and Tomice as described above,

came a greater or lesser distance to an old wolf pit, well marked. Not far from this wolf pit he showed a sufficiently prominent mound having a roundness and a small ditch, that is a crown, around it — which he held to be a boundary mound between Łukomia and Trąbczyn — and beside this mound showed a track branching to the right from the Zagórów and Drzewce road, heading by a shortcut well to the right under the hill towards Łukomia, joining the Osiny road and running together with it further. Then, going on from the mound through pine forest towards the [sandy hill](https://www.google.com/maps/place/52%C2%B007'23.9%22N+17%C2%B052'55.4%22E) above the Osiny road — now the Pyzdry road — he came to the [cross road](https://www.google.com/maps/place/52%C2%B007'24.4%22N+17%C2%B052'53.5%22E) from Osiny running towards Łukomia, or rather to Łukom towards Pyzdry, crossing the Zagórów road here in this place. Having crossed it, he brought his court to the second mound, lying just beyond the Osiny road a few paces from the road, barely raised above the ground, but having a great likeness [to a mound] and a visible small ditch and roundness, upon which there stands a pine stump, already decayed at its edge, concealing the ditch in this place — which he holds to be a boundary mound of Łukomia and Trąbczyn.

From there, going further, he ran his boundary line south along that same Zagórów and Drzewce road to Kalisz, Chocz, and Tomice, passing over a small sandy hill described above, and so going two or three staj, with his own pine forest and Ostrowce woodland to the left, and [the land] now separated to the Ląd Convent through the compromise of 1761 running beside that same Zagórów or Kalisz road to the right — he came to a prominent mound, heaped by the compromise recently as a marker of the pine forest allotted to the Ląd Convent, on the right side of the Zagórów or Kalisz road, crushed by a fallen half-pine. From that mound, going on further along the same road through pine forest to the south and

showing his pine forest to the left and to the right towards Łukomia as before, going likewise two or more staj, he came to a dam, not very long, about one hundred łokci, not very high, and likewise to the [stawisko](https://www.google.com/maps/place/52%C2%B007'15.8%22N+17%C2%B053'01.8%22E) lying between hills and pine forest on the left side, empty, upon which is a sluice in the manner of a small weir. This stawisko or empty marsh, extending no further than one hundred łokci beyond the dam, and thereafter becoming wide, overgrown with clumps and various trees, having no mill race cut below the dam, now dry — and likewise the woodland of various kinds standing and lying around it and beside it, in part not inconsiderable, overturned and dried out.

And likewise the Ostrowce woodland, shown above at the corner mounds, touching on one side there the mounds and on this side here the said stawisko, lying beside the stawisko on this side to the left; and likewise a ditch from the said dam through the Zagórów or Kalisz road towards the Łukomia pond lying below [i.e., to the west] towards Łukomia, passing through from the stawisko both there and through marshland woodland; and likewise the Łukomia pond itself — called the new pond — lying on similar marshes, overgrown with clumps and various trees, having only a mill race cut below the dam and before the mill, sufficiently wide and long; and likewise the dam, well made, two staj long, wide, and high, and the mill on this pond — all of which the Honourable Prusimski, turning aside from his boundary line, showed to his court.

And thereupon at once he demonstrates to his court that by this pond and so great a dam, when the heir of Łukomia built it and the pond on such marshes — similar to the stawisko and lying to the right of the boundary line road belonging to it — they caused the flooding of the said stawisko belonging to Trąbczyn, as the Honourable Prusimski and his people allege, and likewise of the pastures, meadows, and woodlands upon that stawisko — further proving

the ownership of that empty stawisko, that is the empty marshes, belonging to Trąbczyn, he submits:

In the first place the partition among the late Honourable and Honest Wojciech, Mikołaj, and Stanisław Trąbczyński, brothers by birth, heirs of the whole Trąbczyn estate, attested in the Konin municipal court in 1522, on Thursday on the vigil of the Ascension of the Lord — by which partition Stanisław Trąbczyński receives at Nowa Wieś a pond with meadows;

In the second place he submits two decrees of 1589 in the Konin land court under one act, that is on Monday before Saints Vitus and Modestus — between the late Honourable Wojciech Trąbczyński, at that time heir only of a certain portion of the estates of Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały, Osiny, and the woodlands, as plaintiff on the one side, and the late Honourable Tomasz Łukomski and Barbara Brodzińska, widow of Sebastian Modliboski, heirs of the village of Łukomia, as defendants on the other side — concerning the flooding of the woodlands, pine forests, pastures, and meadows on the estate of Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały, Osiny, and the woodlands belonging to his portion, caused by a new pond built on the Łukomia estate, with a judicial site inspection assigned to the ground, ordered to be conducted by the defendant party and not the plaintiff — both issued and made;

And likewise he submits a third decree between the same parties in the Konin land court, issued after those decrees, by which Tomasz Łukomski was ordered to swear, with two noblemen, to the damages caused in the Trąbczyn pine forests, meadows, and pastures in the portion of the said Wojciech Trąbczyński by the flooding of the water.

And likewise he submits a fourth decree of deferral of that same oath for six years, between the same parties in the later year 1595, in the same Konin courts, on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi.

And finally he submits the partition among the Honourables and Honest Andrzej, Jan, and Władysław Trąbczyński, brothers by birth, heirs of the villages of Trąbczyn Mały and Wielki, Osiny, and the woodlands, of the sixth

October 1693, filed in the Pyzdry municipal court — by which partition the said Trąbczyński, dividing the said villages among themselves, apart from the ponds expressed in that partition, reserve to themselves the remaining ponds or rather empty marshes for their common use and fishing, and likewise retain for common pasture the flat marshes overgrown with clumps found in the pine forests, and moreover provide that none of them should take from the serfs what any serf may cut — all of this they stipulate.

And further, proving the ownership of the empty stawisko belonging to Trąbczyn and the encroachment thereon of the Honourable Chełmski, he submits several judicial site inspections and manifests lodged and initiated by the late Honourable Krzysztof Prusimski, heir of Trąbczyn with appurtenances, against the Honourable Chełmski, predecessors of the present party, concerning the appropriation of this stawisko and the Lusnia woodland, the violent felling of the meadows upon it, and the building of the dam on that stawisko — beginning from 1713 —

And likewise the Konin land court decree against the predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski on Monday after the Sunday Septuagesime in 1728, concerning that same stawisko and the transgressions committed upon it, with a field session assigned to the ground;

And likewise all other proceedings expressed in the schedule of the Honourable Starost, signed by his court, he submits — and likewise declares in court that sworn inquiries conducted pursuant to that decree upon the boundaries will be present and will be submitted in the further course of the cause.

And finally, that this stawisko has belonged to Trąbczyn from of old and Trąbczyn has used it — although at times the Honourables and Honest Chełmski encroached upon it — sworn inquiry, old and future, will attest. The Honourable Prusimski also says that since by the partition of 1522

at Nowa Wieś Stanisław Trąbczyński received a pond with meadows, and there is no other pond with or without meadows on the entire estate of Nowa Wieś except this present stawisko, he thereby proves by that partition that this stawisko has become depleted from that pond, and for that reason is called a stawisko or rather empty marshes in the later partition of 1693.

And therefore, from these proofs, holding the empty stawisko, that is the empty marshes, to be properly Trąbczyn's, and taking them into his boundary line, and protesting thereupon concerning the unjust and wrongful usurpation thereof, and concerning the dam and the flooding of meadows, pine forests, and [other land] — referring to the former manifests and judicial site inspections lodged against the heirs of Łukomia — the Honourable Prusimski went further from this place along that same beaten Zagórów or Kalisz road towards the west; and having crossed the ditch beside the very dam of that stawisko and coming out below the hill towards the old Kalisz or Zagórów road lying to the right, and showing at the same time a small field lying on the right side of the road belonging to Łukomia, he said that here beside this road there was a boundary mound but it has been ploughed up by the predecessor of the Honourable Chełmski — and in support submits the judicial site inspection and manifest made in 1726 at Pyzdry.

Going further from there along that same Zagórów road towards the west, leaving a small field to the right of that road belonging to Łukomia or Łomowo and the road peacefully used up to it, and on the other side to the left his own pine forest of pine on strips not very wide beyond the stawisko, which the people assert the former heirs of Trąbczyn forbade Łukomia to sow [into] and [which has] grown up — he came to the old third mound, not very raised above the ground, with a sufficiently good roundness, a sufficiently marked small ditch all around it, that is a crown, lying to the right of the Kalisz or Zagórów road beside a small oak on a slightly sandy spot — which

the heir of Trąbczyn held and said to be the true boundary mound of Łukomia or Łomowo with Trąbczyn. From that mound, going further along that same Zagórów road through pine forest in the same direction for several staj, showing blazes on certain pine trees and his pine forest to the left and [that] belonging to Łukomia or Łomowo to the right of the road, and saying that the pine forest has been used and enclosed by Trąbczyn up to that road — he came to the fourth mound, immediately more prominent than the preceding one, lying in a slightly low spot on the left side of the Zagórów or Kalisz road, having a roundness and a small ditch, that is a crown — holding it to be a boundary mound of the above-mentioned estates. And from that mound, taking the direction south along that same old road, having left the new [road] to the right, going through pine forest across a low area a greater or lesser distance of two staj, and having come out a little onto a low hill of sparse pine — he came to a road not very prominent running from Łomowo across to the olędry, and having crossed this road continued his boundary line along that same old Kalisz road southward, past and along the very headland of a small field a few strips wide and one staj long newly cleared in the pine by a Łomowo serf, as he alleges — he came to a second cross road running from Łomowo and Łukomia to the Trąbczyn olędry and joining the first [road] towards the olędry — and crossing this he came to the fifth mound, larger than the others, not very raised above the ground — holding it to be a boundary mound.

From there, going through very sparse pine forest not far, a greater or lesser distance of one and a half staj, to the right of the Łomowo field, along that same old Kalisz road leaving the new [road] to the right nearer the Łomowo field — going a greater or lesser distance of three staj and saying his pine forest is to the left and [that] belonging to Łukomia is to the right of the road and has been used as such formerly — he came to a drainage strip lying immediately to the left of the Zagórów or Kalisz road and touching the very end of the Kalisz road, and likewise to a prominent road running from Łomowo and

Łukomia by a shortcut across the Kalisz or Zagórów road past the very marsh across, and running towards Królikowo above that same marsh at first and then further away from the marsh through pine — and having crossed this road and gone a short distance he came to a second road also running from Łomowo and Łukomia along the Łomowo field itself towards the Kalisz ford, joining the Kalisz road at a diagonal about a staj above [i.e., to the north of] the ford — and joining the Kalisz road. And from there going along the Kalisz road nearer the Łomowo field, also newly cleared since the stakes upon it are standing, a greater or lesser distance of a staj, he came to the ford — not very great, now dry — called the Kalisz [ford], beside which there are also wooden footboards laid.

Having crossed this ford, he showed the road immediately branching to the right from the ford towards the west, leading to Grądzeń or the glassworks and also to Tomice past Grądzeń — branching higher up towards Grądzeń, as the people say — as expressed in the judicial site inspection, that is the field act conducted by the late Honourable Mieleński, Boundary Surveyor of Poznań, from a Tribunal decree of 1658. Having left that road to the right and turning left, southward, along the Kalisz road still at that same Kalisz ford, and having come out of the ford into pine forest, he showed, not far from that ford to the left of the Kalisz road, a blaze on a pine tree, sufficiently prominent and old — and thereupon submitting the above-mentioned judicial site inspection by the Poznań Boundary Surveyor Mieleński of 1658, proves thereby that the Honourable Trąbczyński, heir of Trąbczyn, having stood beside this Kalisz road, whether here or above before the ford, asserted and acknowledged it to be a boundary between Trąbczyn and Grądzeń, and likewise acknowledged the pine forest to the left of this road as Trąbczyn's and to the right as Grądzeń's. And likewise he proves that although the heir of Łukomia protested at that judicial site inspection against the heir of Trąbczyn — not, however, concerning the road, that it should not be a boundary, but only that he [Trąbczyński] attributed the boundary not to Łukomia but to Grądzeń.

And likewise the Ląd Convent, during that judicial site inspection and thereafter always held

that same road as a boundary, and established its Grądzeń boundary up to that road — although he now has it not with the Convent but with Łukomia and Łomowo. The Honourable Prusimski says this is because the Convent, having agreed to a compromise with the Honourable Chełmski after that judicial site inspection and after the three field decrees in 1761, voluntarily ceded that boundary and that ground from that road to the Honourable Chełmski, and allowed it to be marked above with mounds through that compromise. And that this is so he submits the same compromise decree between those same parties — made without his court appearance and that of other corner neighbours — in the same year 1761, filed in the Kalisz municipal court on Monday after the Sunday Cantate — which, having been made without his court appearance, cannot prejudice his former boundary and that old boundary road, nor can it abolish and alter the former Trąbczyn boundary. And he further says that although he now has no boundary with Grądzeń but with Łukomia, this makes no difference to Trąbczyn, whether to have a boundary with Grądzeń or with Łukomia — for Grądzeń is a portion of the Łukomia or Łomowo ground, and lies within and within the boundaries of Łukomia, sold off and separated to the Ląd Convent by Bochneta, heir of Łukomia and Łomowo.

And moreover, since the heir of Łukomia and Łomowo, protesting at that judicial site inspection against Trąbczyński — that he had attributed the boundary to Grądzeń and not to Łukomia — himself wished to have and acknowledged that Trąbczyn boundary with Łukomia and not with Grądzeń — and accordingly, pursuant to that same protest and acknowledgement of the heir of Łukomia, and the cession of ground by the Convent to Łukomia, and pursuant to the general boundaries of Łukomia or Łomowo determined by the Kalisz road — the Honourable Prusimski, wishing to have his boundary not with Grądzeń but with Łukomia, and not disputing with him the boundary or the ground ceded — is willing to concede it to him together with

the pine forest to the right of the Kalisz road belonging to Łukomia or Łomowo, and his own to the left belonging to Trąbczyn; and likewise acknowledging and asserting the Kalisz road as a boundary — insofar as it is confirmed by both corner and boundary mounds and blazes shown in the boundary line from the very corner mound all the way to this point, and by the long-standing use of the pine forest up to that road from of old by Trąbczyn — he went further from this place between Trąbczyn and Łukomia or Łomowo along that same Kalisz road southward about two staj, and brought his court to a ditch and a small bridge over the ditch, dug and placed, as he alleges, not very long ago by the Honourable Chełmski — whereupon the Honourable Prusimski protests here at once that this ditch, lying on the other side of the road to the left, was dug not very long ago in his absence on his own ground and in his own pine forest, and a small bridge placed upon it. Having crossed this bridge and going along that same road in one and the same direction southward and showing blazes on the pine trees, a greater or lesser distance of two staj, he came to the road from Zagórów and other villages running to Tomice, branching diagonally to the right from the beaten Kalisz road. And from there, going further along the Kalisz road southward, he came to a second cross beaten road from Trąbczyn, formerly through pine and now through the Trąbczyn olędry, to Tomice — running beside the Pyzdry corner mounds lying a half-mile from here — and joining the first Tomice road mentioned above a few staj from here below the hill, as the people maintain — beside which road he showed an old cross cut in a pine tree, standing to the left of the Kalisz road.

Having crossed this cross road of Trąbczyn or Tomice, the Honourable Prusimski ran his boundary line further between Trąbczyn and Łukomia or Łomowo through pine along that same Kalisz road, showing his pine forest to the left, long used and enclosed by Trąbczyn, and demonstrating blazes on pine trees; and leaving the pine forest lying to the right belonging to Łukomia or Łomowo — he came to a small drainage strip or small ford from the duck bogs, crossing through the Kalisz road to another drainage strip lying on the other side of the Kalisz road, and likewise to a road lying beside this drainage strip running from the Trąbczyn olędry

above the duck bogs [i.e., to the north of the duck bogs], joining the Kalisz road diagonally from the left — and also a road now newly made through the Kalisz road in that direction. Going also along these tracks, he showed blazes on the pine trees. Having crossed this drainage strip, ford, and road, he ran his boundary line further along the long drainage strip called the duck bogs lying close to the Kalisz road, along that same Kalisz road through pine southward, for several staj, and came to a small ravine cut by water from the duck bogs to other drainage strips lying on the other side of the Kalisz road. Having crossed this ravine, he showed an old pine tree with two blazes, forked, standing immediately beside this ravine and the Kalisz road to the left, on what appears to be a round and raised spot — which pine tree he held to be grown upon a [former] mound, and asserted that a boundary mound is actually here. From that ravine and the forked pine, going further along that same old Kalisz road to the left, leaving the newly made [road] to the right, and going several staj toward Studzienka below the hills there and towards those hills called — as they say — the tavern hills — when the Honourable Prusimski came straight opposite eight mounds which people call Cygany (Gypsies), lying one and a half staj from the boundary line and the Kalisz road — as to the first mound taken — towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród to the east, above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of the Lusnia woodland], long and now so called and named so by the Honourable Chełmski himself, stretching there towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród, and lying on this side of the Lusnia woodland with respect to the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski —

the Honourable Prusimski, having come in this place opposite the above-mentioned mounds, standing five staj before Studzienka below the hills called — as they say — the tavern hills, and likewise before those hills — pausing a little to show further and determine his terminal point of his boundary with Łukomia or Łomowo, which he establishes at Studzienka and the tavern hills — still wishing to show here the further continuation of the boundary and his further boundary line, and the accuracy of what has already been run, and likewise the ownership of the grounds, pine forests, and woodlands — Ostrowce, Miąsykąt,

Dziadulina, and in particular the one called Lusnia — and likewise the empty stawisko, that is the empty marshes described above, taken into his boundary line and lying within his boundary line, and taken by the Honourable Chełmski in his boundary line — and likewise the unlawfulness of the judicial site inspection of 1592 as contrary to all documents expressed below, and in particular as contrary to the Lusnia woodland and the corner mound or convergence of Biskupice with Łukomia and Trąbczyn expressed in it — wishing to show, prove, and test all this to his court both by these mounds and blazes behind them, and above all by other documents and decrees regarding the eight newly and freshly heaped mounds and forty-one blazes made — he therefore requested his court for the review and description of those mounds and blazes. The court, wishing to inform itself in this cause as thoroughly and precisely as possible, permitted this — and accordingly the Honourable Prusimski, having left the said Kalisz road and his boundary line, brought his court one and a half staj from the Kalisz road to the first of these eight mounds, lying in low ground beside a small drainage strip, sufficiently prominent and evident, having a roundness and a crown, overgrown with heather, lying at a distance in this place from the Lusnia woodland on this side of the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski in the pine forest to the east towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród. And from that mound, going further also to the east towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród a considerable distance, he showed his court seven more such prominent and visible mounds in the pine forest above the said Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of it] — at first one and a half staj further, then at a staj from that same Lusnia woodland — lying one after another at various intervals of three, four, five staj, stretching toward the corduroy crossings for several staj, occupying and taking in the whole Lusnia woodland. And likewise he showed the last mound of these eight called Cygany — that is the eighth — lying one staj wider from the place where the Honourable Chełmski marked out and formed his corner mound at the very Lusnia woodland itself, and further and above that place and corner mound towards the east towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród, reaching beyond and enclosing that place and corner mound. And likewise, after the conclusion of those mounds,

leading his court further to the east towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród for several staj, he showed his court forty-one blazes on pine trees — which mounds and blazes, that they are on proper Trąbczyn ground, freshly and newly heaped and made from the corduroy crossings above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., at the northern end of the Lusnia woodland] at the great expense and harm of the Trąbczyn ground extending still considerably further — he proves by the following documents, namely:

First, a manifest together with a judicial site inspection by the late Honourable Anna née Słończyc Trąbczyńska, widow of the late Stanisław Trąbczyński, and likewise of the Honourable Maciej Dybryński, possessors of the villages and estates of Trąbczyn, against the late Honourables Zofia née Złotka Kożuchowska, mother, and her sons Andrzej and Jan Kożuchowski, heirs of Biskupice and others — concerning the armed incursion onto Trąbczyn ground and the transgressions attempted there, and concerning forty-one blazes made on pine trees beginning from the corduroy crossings above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., at its northern end], freshly made, and eight mounds newly and freshly heaped on the ground — lodged in the Pyzdry municipal court on Saturday before Holy Trinity in 1644; and thereafter a default decree (default) in the same matter at the suit of the said Honourable Anna née Słończyc, remaining widow, and of Marcin, Wojciech, Władysław, and Andrzej Trąbczyński, and likewise Maciej Dybryński, heirs and possessors of the villages of Trąbczyn with appurtenances — against the said Honourables Kożuchowska, mother and sons, and Łukasz Biskupski, heirs of the villages of Biskupice and Bystrzyce — in the Konin land court on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi of the same year.

And likewise he submits a second decree in the same Konin land court in the same matter between these parties, in the same year on Monday after Saint Hedwig, with a motion to the Tribunal admitted by the plaintiff party, that is the Trąbczyńskis, after a delay to the documents granted to the defendant party as unnecessary in an action of fact.

He further submits a Piotrków Tribunal decree in the same matter between these parties, on Wednesday on the very feast of Saint Prisca, Virgin and Martyr, of 1645 — by which a judicial site inspection and sworn inquiry upon the ground of the alleged injury is assigned, to be conducted by the Kalisz Boundary Surveyor within six weeks, with the appointed date in the Tribunal reserved for the further adjudication of the cause. And likewise he submits the recognition of Jan Masłek of Osiny, Court Summoner, and of the Honourable Andrzej Wejski, nobleman, at the instance of the Honourables Trąbczyński, against the Honourables Kożuchowski and Biskupski, heirs of Biskupice and Bystrzyce, in the Pyzdry municipal court on Thursday before the Sunday called Invocavit of 1645 — in which they state that the Honourable Trąbczyński was prepared pursuant to the Tribunal decree to conduct the judicial site inspection concerning the making of forty-one blazes and the heaping of eight mounds, and that they attempted and were on the ground — but since neither the other party nor the Boundary Surveyor had arrived, they declared this in the municipal court. And likewise he submits a second Piotrków Tribunal decree between those same parties in the same matter, in the same year 1645 on the Monday called Reminiscere — by which a second judicial site inspection and sworn inquiry on the ground of the alleged injury is ordered, with the restoration of the appointed date in the Tribunal. Then further he submits a third Piotrków Tribunal decree between those same parties in the same matter, from sworn inquiries and the judicial site inspection conducted by the Boundary Surveyor, of 1646, on Thursday after the Feast of the Immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin Mary — by which Łukasz Biskupski, having incurred the penalties, was ordered to swear in the Konin land court with two noblemen to the said forty-one blazes made on pine trees and eight mounds newly and freshly heaped — while the Kożuchowskis were released.

[Translator's note: Quadendo paenas succubitas: asterisked in the source as uncertain. The phrase appears to describe the penalty incurred; succubitas does not correspond to standard Latin but may be a distorted form of succubiturus (having incurred / about to undergo). Translated according to the most plausible sense.]

And likewise he submits the Konin land court decree between the same Honourables and Honest Trąbczyński and Łukasz Biskupski, from that Tribunal decree,

of 1647, on Monday before Saint Hedwig — by which, pursuant to the formula in the Tribunal decree, he was ordered to swear; and having performed it and sworn off the said marks and mounds — as not made and heaped by him — although alone without two noblemen — was from any further proceedings acquitted.

Having thus submitted these documents and actually shown to his court the mounds described above and the blazes above the said Lusnia woodland, and having shown and remonstrated to his court the position and actual existence of them — the Honourable Prusimski says and proves in his court by those same mounds, blazes, and documents: that since these mounds and blazes actually, according to these documents, are above the Lusnia woodland — so and not otherwise called by the people and by the Honourable Chełmski himself — and moreover lie still remote, at a staj from the Lusnia woodland, and the whole of that Lusnia woodland, and likewise Dziadulina, Miąsykąt, the stawisko or empty marshes, and the Ostrowce woodland described above, lie in the middle of his boundary line or within his boundary line, taken into his boundary line — and finally the whole boundary line and corner mounds of the Honourable Chełmski reach and enclose them — and moreover the last Cygany mound, that is the eighth, lying further about a staj to the east towards the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród, reaches above and beyond that place and the corner mound established and formed by the Honourable Chełmski at the very Lusnia woodland, and that mound together with its other companions and the blazes stretching further toward the corduroy crossings beyond the last mound above the Lusnia woodland, as shown in the remonstration — and likewise documents attest the further extent of the Lusnia woodland and other Trąbczyn ground in length, and also in breadth on the other side, further from the mounds towards the south —

and these mounds, blazes, and the documents submitted above set forth that they were newly and freshly heaped and made, beginning from the corduroy crossings above the Lusnia woodland, on proper Trąbczyn ground, to the considerable detriment and harm of Trąbczyn ground still extending considerably further. Therefore the Honourable Prusimski says that since these mounds and blazes

are actually above the Lusnia woodland and still lie remote from that woodland, those same mounds and blazes — apart from all other proofs — demonstrate not only the ground on which they themselves lie, but also the ground between them and the Lusnia woodland, and finally the entire Lusnia woodland, Dziadulina, Miąsykąt, Ostrowce, and the stawisko or empty marshes — in short, the entire and complete ground and the olędry unjustly taken and claimed by the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski and lying within his boundary line — as enclosing and establishing the actual substance and ownership of all these, and as they must necessarily and are Trąbczyn's — sufficiently prove and set forth. And finally not only these themselves prove the ownership and substance of the grounds and woodlands expressed above, but also demonstrate more broadly still, in breadth and in length, the extent of his Trąbczyn grounds and pine forests, and likewise the further continuation of the boundary line and boundary with Łukomia or Łomowo — halted opposite these mounds on the Kalisz road — and which must extend all the way to Studzienka and the hills and tavern bogs to the corner mound there established. And moreover the Honourable Prusimski says that these mounds and blazes not only assert and confirm everything expressed above, but finally approve and corroborate the whole boundary line run to the place described above in its entirety, and likewise everything within the boundary line his — and assert and acknowledge his whole boundary line as accurate and good, confirmed by documents both written and on the ground by mounds and blazes, and likewise by long-standing use — while on the contrary, taking those same mounds and blazes, they overturn and rebuke the entire and general boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski as contrary to those mounds and blazes, and as formed and marked through the proper grounds, pine forests, and woodlands and the stawisko, that is the empty marshes of Trąbczyn — and likewise his corner mounds established and formed on Trąbczyn ground, and the grounds wrongly appropriated by him to himself and his reaching to Biskupice, and likewise the judicial site inspection of 1592 as contrary in all respects to these mounds, proofs, and documents submitted above and below.

And finally, that the Honourable Chełmski through

the Trąbczyn boundary, confirmed by so many proofs, according to the above-mentioned judicial site inspection of 1592, can neither reach the boundary nor the corner mound of Biskupice — the aforesaid proofs and the Trąbczyn ground and boundary standing in the way — and ought not to — and cannot have a corner mound with Biskupice — all this is expressed and set forth not only by those mounds and blazes, but also by the Łukomia heirs' own judicial site inspection of 1593, made in the Pyzdry municipal court on Thursday on the very feast of Saint Mary Magdalene — one year after the above-mentioned judicial site inspection — which sets forth that the heirs, conducting it, whether going themselves or wishing to lead [a party] to the Biskupice boundary or corner mound, could reach Biskupice by no other means than by the boundaries of Osiny — these are the words of that same judicial site inspection — and the boundaries of Osiny are the same as those of Trąbczyn.

And to this, further proving in that place the unlawfulness and great doubtfulness and the contrariness of the judicial site inspection of 1589, conducted in the field and attested in 1592, to the documents as well as to the mounds expressed above — the Honourable Prusimski says and brings before his court: that this judicial site inspection was conducted not by the heir of all of Trąbczyn but only by Wojciech Trąbczyński, who had only a portion thereof, past the other co-heirs, his four nephews, then with him co-heirs of Trąbczyn — and moreover that it was conducted by the guardian of those nephews to their detriment — and proving likewise the following circumstances, the Honourable Prusimski says again and brings before his court, along with the documents expressed below: that since this judicial site inspection of 1592 was ordered to be conducted not by the plaintiff, that is Wojciech Trąbczyński, but by the defendants, that is the heirs of Łukomia — by four Kalisz land decrees — and it was conducted and attested at the instance of Wojciech Trąbczyński as plaintiff, it may be that the heir of Łukomia conducted it at his instance, without the presence and knowledge

of the same, and had it attested to the detriment of him and of the Trąbczyn grounds. Secondly, that this judicial site inspection has something in it — namely, that it was conducted in the field in 1589 around the Feast of Saint James, and only three years later, in 1592 in the Konin municipal court around the Feast of the Immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin Mary, was attested. Even if it were granted from the outset, even if it had been conducted by Wojciech Trąbczyński himself and attested at his instance — it is not fitting: for Wojciech Trąbczyński at that time was not alone heir of all of Trąbczyn, but together with his four nephews, heir of Trąbczyn, that is the whole Trąbczyński estate — as the judicial site inspection itself shows. When this judicial site inspection was conducted in 1589, that Wojciech Trąbczyński was at that time guardian of his four nephews; then, having ceased to be guardian of those his nephews, he at once began a lawsuit with them in 1591 — and so while that lawsuit was ongoing between them, only then the attestation of this judicial site inspection in 1592 in the Konin municipal court to the detriment of his nephews and his own wards — and possibly by prearrangement and previously arranged [arrangement] with the heirs of Łukomia as well — took place. For the said Wojciech Trąbczyński, having a lawsuit with them concerning the flooding of the Trąbczyn and Osiny grounds, pine forests, and woodlands, having obtained a decree against the heirs of Łukomia ordering an oath or rather a swearing-off of the damages — as attested by the Konin land court decree of 1595 — limited that same oath for six years.

And therefore, since this judicial site inspection was made and conducted in such a manner — by an heir of only a portion, Wojciech Trąbczyński — and it is not clear whether even by himself — contrary to law and all proofs both written and shown on the ground, contrary to the position of the villages and places, past the common heirs and his nephews and wards, to their detriment, and likewise to the detriment of the old boundaries — and one might say privately and without any decree since it was not ordered for the plaintiff — and moreover since not immediately upon its very conduct

but only three years after its conduct in the field, in the Konin municipal court — and at that during a lawsuit and quarrel with his nephews — it was attested — it is therefore shown to be not only subject to great doubt, but also contrary to all proofs and to the law itself, and by reason of the statutory time-limit provisions of the law governing it, of no worth, and indeed reprehensible and abolished by the law itself. And moreover, that this judicial site inspection is ordered to be observed only insofar as it has not been and does not prove contrary to other proofs — by the last decree of the Bydgoszcz Tribunal of 1768.

And so neither the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski and the claims to the grounds taken or marked by his boundary line — and in particular as to the corner mound and convergence of Biskupice, to which he can only reach by necessarily passing through the Trąbczyn grounds and boundary, as he now does — can find support in this judicial site inspection, nor ought it to; nor can the Honourable Chełmski establish for himself boundaries and corner mounds within the Trąbczyn grounds and boundary on the foundation of it — of the nullity of which and in respect of which the Honourable Prusimski, having protested in his court from the reason and knowledge of those prejudices now formed from the documents and position of places — submits in proof and confirmation of all that is expressed above the following documents:

And in the first place four Kalisz land decrees in the Konin land court on Monday before Saint Vitus of 1589, between Wojciech Trąbczyński as plaintiff on the one side and the heirs of Łukomia as defendants on the other, concerning the flooding of the grounds, meadows, pine forests, and woodlands on the Trąbczyn and Osiny estates caused by a newly built pond, dam, and the holding back of water on the Łukomia estate — by which he demonstrates to his court that the judicial site inspection assigned by those decrees was ordered not for the plaintiff but for the defendant party.

And likewise, secondly, he submits a decree of acquittal concerning the digging of a ditch and the ploughing up of a beaten road — between the same Honourable Wojciech, uncle and guardian, and Wawrzyniec, Rachwall, Walenty, and Stanisław, his nephews Trąbczyński, on the one side, and the late Honourable Mikołaj Trąbczyński, heir of a portion of Szetlew, on the other — in the same Konin land court, in the same year and under the same act, made three years before the judicial site inspection — by which he proves that Wojciech Trąbczyński was heir not of all of Trąbczyn but was guardian of his above-mentioned nephews, co-heirs of Trąbczyn with him.

And likewise he submits the above-mentioned judicial site inspection of that same year 1589, on Monday on the vigil of Saint James the Apostle, conducted in the field pursuant to the above-mentioned four decrees from the portion of the Honourable Trąbczyński himself, and attested in the Konin municipal court on Thursday after the Feast of the Immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin Mary of 1592 — by which he likewise demonstrates to his court that Wojciech Trąbczyński was not heir of all of Trąbczyn, and that this judicial site inspection was made during guardianship and attested during the lawsuit and quarrel over the partition occurring with him in the years expressed above.

And likewise he submits the partition among the said Wawrzyniec, Rachwall, Walenty, and Stanisław Trąbczyński, nephews, by then released from guardianship, on the one side, and the Honourable late Wojciech Trąbczyński, their uncle, on the other — concerning the division of Trąbczyn with appurtenances — of 1591, on Monday after the Sunday Jubilate — made nearly two years after the judicial site inspection was conducted in the field and three quarters of a year before its attestation — attested in the Konin municipal court by the Burgrave — by which he likewise proves that Wojciech Trąbczyński was not heir of all of Trąbczyn;

Both after that judicial site inspection conducted in the field and before its attestation, [as] he was in a lawsuit with his nephews. And likewise, proving that even after this judicial site inspection there was no single ownership in Trąbczyn with appurtenances, he submits the decree of 1595 in the Konin Kalisz land court on Monday after Saint Hedwig — between the same Wojciech Trąbczyński and the Ląd Convent, concerning the flooding by the Tarszew mill

at Kopanina of the Trąbczyn meadows belonging to his portion, with the damage [podatek od] relating to his portion alone, apart from the other co-heirs with him.

And likewise he submits the agreement between the late Honourable and Honest Szczęsna née Popów, widow of the late Honourable Jan Trąbczyński, as mother, on the one side, and the Honourables Rachwall, Walenty, and Stanisław, her sons Trąbczyński, on the other — concerning the allotment of a portion in the Trąbczyński estate — made upon ratification in the Konin municipal court on Monday after the Exaltation of the Holy Cross of 1596, and filed.
