And likewise he submits the partition concerning the division of the same Trąbczyński estate between the Honourables late Andrzej, Jan, and Władysław Trąbczyński, brothers by birth, of 1693 — already placed above, extracted from the Pyzdry municipal court records.

And finally the Honourable Prusimski says that the predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski never used this judicial site inspection at the past sessions and judicial site inspections, and Biskupice never asserted any claim to the Lusnia woodland nor established a corner mound with Łukomia — for Biskupice has no convergence and no claim to the Lusnia woodland, nor any corner mound with Łukomia, either here or in any other place given that wall Trąbczyna — but they have their corner mound with Łazy and Trąbczyn Wielki, near Czarny Bród and the mound called Tomal, lying above [i.e., to the north of] the Siedleska or Jarocin road.

And therefore the Honourable Chełmski wrongly and unjustly establishes and forms for himself a corner mound as though of Łukomia, Trąbczyn, and Biskupice at the Lusnia woodland, and wrongly takes three mounds or stumps from among six lying together in that place — having no likeness whatsoever to mounds, not even round but rather oblong — as his corner mounds; and wrongly brings and draws the heir of Biskupice — thinking as one with him and taking a lead against Trąbczyn — to acknowledge those mounds or corner mounds established by him, and likewise to

claims to proper Trąbczyn grounds, and thereby to dispute. Concerning all this, and concerning the wrongly formed corner mound from three stumps — which three others similar are beside them — and concerning the whole boundary line formed and marked through the proper grounds and woodlands of Trąbczyn belonging and used from of old by Trąbczyn, and for several decades cleared and occupied without any objection by the olędry in the time of his predecessors, and finally concerning the unjust taking and seizure by that same boundary line of the Lusnia, Dziadulina, Miąsykąt, and Ostrowce woodlands, and likewise the empty stawisko or empty marshes, and likewise twelve olędry with inn, fields, and meadows — in short, against the entire boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski as unjust and wrongly and unlawfully formed and marked against law and justice — and likewise for its nullity; and thereupon also at the same time against the Honourable Gałecki, Starost of Bydgoszcz, heir of Biskupice and the Bystrzyce deserted lands: that he, notwithstanding the above-mentioned reasons and documents and the swearing-off of the mounds and blazes by the former heir of Biskupice, having no claim to the Lusnia woodland and no convergence with Łukomia and being unable to have one — proceeding wrongly and unjustly to the detriment and harm of Trąbczyn ground in league with the Honourable Chełmski — wrongly and without any documents, from memory alone, acknowledges in particular the corner mound established by the Honourable Chełmski wrongly and contrary to the above-mentioned documents and mounds at the very Lusnia woodland; and thereby intends to appropriate and [to take] from Trąbczyn the rest of the Lusnia woodland still left by the Honourable Chełmski, and likewise the ground between the Lusnia woodland and the mounds and blazes — and on the other side acknowledges to the Honourable Chełmski the ground and pine forest running from here toward the Kalisz road above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of the Lusnia woodland] together with the Cygany mounds — and thus both of them divide Trąbczyn's proper ground between themselves and content each other at Trąbczyn's expense. After this inspection and description of the eight mounds and blazes

described above, and the remonstration made to his court and the proofs and protests submitted above — the Honourable Prusimski, having returned to his boundary line and the Kalisz road and having come to the place where his boundary line was halted — says and proves to his court by the above-mentioned documents: that since in those documents it is expressed that the said mounds and blazes were heaped and made with great detriment and harm to Trąbczyn ground — and these are actually shown at the present appointed date — it follows without any question that the Trąbczyn ground must extend further in breadth to the south and in length to the east.

And it therefore follows that the further boundary line of Trąbczyn with Łukomia or Łomowo must still continue, and must have its determination of the terminal point at the old corner mounds or convergence of three estates at the tavern hills and bogs and Studzienka established from of old — as expressed in Mieleński's judicial site inspection — and accordingly the Honourable Prusimski from this place continued his further boundary line, or rather boundary with Łukomia or Łomowo, southward along that same old Kalisz road, leaving the new [road] to the right, about five staj through pure somewhat sparse pine, and showing blazes on the pine trees and likewise the Rozciszew folwark of the Honourable Chełmski, his inherited property lying in the pine on the right side of the boundary line and Kalisz road a greater or lesser distance of six staj from here, visible from here — came to Studzienka, not very deep or wide, faced with no stonework, which is said to have been formerly faced with brick but demolished by the heirs of Łukomia — and likewise to the long hills lying respectively cross to the boundary line through the Kalisz road at one end between east and south towards the Jarocin road running from Szetlew and likewise towards Gąsiennice for

[Translator's note: Nieocembrowana: having no cembrowanie, that is no lining or stonework. The word refers to the stone or timber lining of a well, spring, or cistern. Translated as "faced with no stonework".]

several staj, and at the other end towards the Rozciszew folwark to the west for about three staj — lying immediately beside that Studzienka. And likewise the drainage strips on the other side of the hills on both sides of the Kalisz road, close to those hills, through which the Kalisz road passes and beyond the hills runs between the drainage strips — which hills and bogs the heir and people of Trąbczyn say, pursuant to Mieleński's judicial site inspection of 1658 by the Poznań Boundary Surveyor, are called the tavern hills and bogs — and that they hold this name from two inns or taverns, the ruins of which are still extant and were shown to his court: one, the Królikowo [tavern], above the Kalisz road; and the other, the Oborsk [tavern], above the Królikowo road to Toporów, Tomice, and further, lying in the pine about a half-mile from here. And likewise in that same place immediately beyond the hills on the side where the bogs lie, the Honourable Prusimski showed his court a large stone, three łokci long, not very raised above the ground, lying beside the Kalisz road — holding it to be a guardian of the corner mound. And likewise he showed the road there from that stone from the Kalisz road branching to the left westward towards Rozciszew, running beside the drainage strip alongside the above-described hills to the left and on to Rozciszew — prominent and old. And likewise he showed, straight opposite Studzienka beside the very Kalisz road, a large old pine tree with an old burnt cross cut into it standing there — and on the other side of Studzienka he showed a boggy marsh lying about half a staj from Studzienka below the hills to the left, about three staj long, not very wide, overgrown with various trees and clumps.

Having thus shown these marks to his court and establishing here his terminal point or corner mound, he says that here is the confluence of three estates, namely

his Trąbczyn to the left and Łukomia to the right of the road — and that both these estates have used pine forest up to this point — and of a third estate, having no definitive document at present as to which third estate should reach here — especially since no corner neighbours from Tomice or Biskupice appeared, and they are to be treated in default — he held to be Biskupice, and placed here the corner mound of these three estates, namely Trąbczyn, Łukomia or Łomowo, and Biskupice, and determined his boundary with Łukomia or Łomowo with a reservation of his legal rights regarding the other estates.

After this boundary line run and determined at the terminal point at Studzienka and the above-described tavern hills — as to the Trąbczyn boundary with Łukomia or Łomowo — although the Honourable Chełmski does not appear in refutation of the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski and to show his own, and is to be treated in default — together with the corner neighbours — the court, wishing to inform itself as thoroughly as possible in all respects in the judging and establishing of this cause and the boundary lines of both parties — especially since the Honourable Prusimski requests this and asks his court for the execution and inspection of the boundary line and its description — the court therefore offers to proceed to the review of the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski, insofar as it has been marked out. And meanwhile, having suspended for a time the review of the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski — since the matter is proceeding in a split court — and since the Honourable Prusimski, having been issued by the court a judicial declaration for the bringing of a sworn surveyor to produce a map, brought in the course of his boundary line a sworn surveyor, namely the Reverend Father Benedykt Woronowski, Doctor of Theology and Philosophy and actual Professor of Philosophy at the Poznań Academy —

the court, having informed itself from his instrument and oath that he is sworn, orders that the said Most Reverend Father Woronowski, surveyor, delineate as precisely as possible the boundary line of both parties, already run in one Trąbczyn boundary, and likewise the inspection of the mounds and blazes, and the position of all adjacent places and other places even outside the boundary lines — shown at various sessions before and needed for the remonstration the present boundary lines —

during the course of the present commission, and form a map — and to present it to his court for its verification and signing, at the determination of that commission, to the Most Honourable citizen President. And this immediately without delay. After which sentence promulgated by his court, the present commission court, resolving the above-mentioned suspension, proceeded and went to the review and description of the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski, insofar as it had been marked out with stakes.

[Section 5: Review of Chełmski's Boundary Line — Trąbczyn–Biskupice Boundary — Sworn Inquiry — Resolution on the Starting Point]

And that this road between Trąbczyn, Drzewce, Łukomia, Wrąbczyn, and Zagórów, as the judicial site inspection states, can by no means go and does not go in any way other than as immediately described to his court — this he submitted and in that name protested. And likewise he showed his court the Ostrowce woodland — his own, already shown in his boundary line at his corner mound and explained by the documents there submitted — belonging to Trąbczyn from of old. And likewise beside that same road a few paces he showed a prominent mound — the same as the others — one being #14 of the Drzewce and Trąbczyn wall mounds, and another, that is #15, likewise a Drzewce wall mound with Trąbczyn — lying with a road between them a half staj apart — which the Honourable Chełmski takes as his corner mounds. And likewise he showed a very small hillock

on which a pine stands, or rather a rise of ground swollen up with a pine, lying about four paces from the first mound — which the Honourable Chełmski at past sessions, as the field decrees attest, always took and held to be the third corner mound — but now, having abandoned that hillock, takes in its place an obvious stump as the third corner mound, lying nearly one staj from those two, above the Ostrowce woodland toward the stawisko — which the court, seeing it shown, considers from what was observed on the ground to be a stump. And likewise in refutation of — that these two mounds, #14 and #15, lying eighty łokci apart, taken as corner mounds by the Honourable Chełmski, are not corner mounds but are actually wall mounds of Drzewce with Trąbczyn — and that the two mounds below #14, and further the others, lie at the same distance from one another as those two, while the others lie variously and unevenly as described below — he therefore for this purpose showed his court, going from here toward his own corner mound westward, along the former boundary of the Honourable Chełmski formed supposedly between Drzewce and Łukomia at two sessions in 1759 and 1760 and now abandoned: mounds of the same form and appearance as those two —

the first from #14 at eighty łokci;

the second after it at seventy łokci;

the third after that at one hundred łokci;

the fourth at one hundred and twenty łokci;

the fifth at eighty łokci;

the sixth likewise at eighty łokci;

the seventh at one hundred łokci;

the eighth at one hundred and forty łokci;

the ninth at two hundred łokci;

the tenth at one hundred and forty łokci;

the eleventh at two hundred łokci;

the twelfth, thirteenth, and fourteenth at one hundred and forty łokci each —

as shown by measurement with an eight-łokieć measuring staff — lying there and held and taken by the Honourable Chełmski at past sessions as wall mounds supposedly in a wall between Drzewce and Łukomia, reaching to his corner mound.

From that corner mound, going further south above the Ostrowce woodland — which the Honourable Prusimski says has been so called from of old and now, and submits the judicial site inspection of 1644 upon it and the proceeding expressed above — for several staj, he showed his court two hillocks, bulbous and elongated, more resembling fallen trees than mounds, and having no resemblance to mounds whatsoever, neither roundness nor ditches — one on which a birch stands, and the other beside a pine running toward the stawisko — marked with stakes. And going further from there he came to the stawisko itself or empty marshes, where likewise above that stawisko he showed a third hillock of the same kind as the two described above. From there going further south through the empty stawisko or marshes for about three staj:

he showed his court shrubs, large clumps, and a great number of various trees both standing and fallen, and the meadows and pastures upon it. And likewise he showed his court that those marshes or stawisko are uniform throughout and stretch toward Osiny past Łazy, through the olędry to Czarny Bród — and there is no watershed in this place where the other party's boundary line runs, nor is any apparent — and that this stawisko lies within the position of Trąbczyn Mały and Osiny, behind the Ostrowce woodland, and has belonged to Trąbczyn from of old — in proof of their ownership submitting the two Trąbczyn partitions, one of 1522 in the Konin [court] and a second of 1693 in the Pyzdry municipal court, and likewise the numerous judicial site inspections and protests of the former heirs of Trąbczyn expressed in his boundary line. He likewise shows the boundary line through that stawisko — quite wide, cut with no small damage to the trees themselves.

And likewise going further and leaving the stawisko, he showed the meadows above the stawisko, and the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski through those meadows, not far from an olędry cottage standing to the left of the boundary line toward Osiny. Going further from there and coming out onto a burnt [zgorzyłe] area between the marshes, he showed a small field worked and held by the Trąbczyn olędry for more than thirty years.

From that small field going south for about three staj through the large marshes, overgrown from of old with clumps and shrubs and belonging to Nowa Wieś and Osiny, he brought his court to the olędry fields — which fields the olędry have peacefully cleared and worked for themselves over a period of about thirty years in the time of the predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski, and those predecessors of the Honourable Chełmski said nothing to them about it and did not encroach on that ground. Going further from there through the olędry field, he brought his court to the road from Trąbczyn to Tomice — formerly through pine and now through the olędry. Having crossed this road, and going through a low or marshy area through the olędry fields, directing himself between east and south, to the left from south, he showed his court an elongated hillock having no resemblance to a mound, neither roundness nor ditch, marked as a mound, lying between the olędry cottages — to the right that of the Diligent Kinos and to the left that of the Diligent Meller. And going further from there through the olędry field between east and south, he came to a sandy hill on which a Lutheran school stands. Leaving that school on the hill to the left and taking part of the cemetery beside it on the right, and going through that cemetery, he showed his court the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski along that same hill bending very sharply between east and south; and on that hill showed a spot with not the slightest resemblance to a mound, marked as a mound. Going from there still more between east and south for several staj along that same not-very-large and ploughed-up hill, he came to the cottage of the Diligent Betka, the settler. Having taken that cottage into his boundary line to the right, he turned along that same hill straight west. Going from there about two staj along that same hill and having come to some young pines [chojary] on that hill, he turned left by a shortcut through fields and strips again between south and west — and going a greater or lesser distance of four staj, he came through meadows to the marshy Lusnia woodland — one staj wide — and having reached the other side of the Lusnia woodland, at that same Lusnia woodland, twenty paces from the woodland and one staj from the Cygany mounds:

he showed his court the corner mound formed by the Honourable Chełmski, and six hillocks or stumps or corner mounds lying together in a heap — elongated, having no roundness and not even any ditches around them, and not the slightest resemblance to mounds — of which three are marked as corner mounds. He reports that the Honourable Chełmski, heir of Łukomia and Łomowo, and likewise Gałecki, Starost of Bydgoszcz, heir of Biskupice, acting as one to the detriment of Trąbczyn, taking those three hillocks or stumps as corner mounds, establish a corner mound here in this place and acknowledge it to each other — which they do as unjustly and wrongly as it is contrary to all proofs. The Honourable Prusimski likewise shows his court in refutation of those their supposed hillocks and corner mound three other hillocks or stumps. And moreover, reminding his court of the documents submitted above at the remonstration of the Cygany mounds and blazes, he says: that if the documents assert and show the eight Cygany mounds and the blazes to have been newly and freshly made on Trąbczyn ground above the Lusnia, and these are actually here above the Lusnia — and moreover since the heir of Biskupice, through swearing off those mounds and blazes, acknowledged the ground to be Trąbczyn's and made no claim to that ground at that time and did not defend himself to the effect that it was Biskupice ground, and Trąbczyn used that ground together with the mounds and the Lusnia woodland —

therefore as the ground on which the mounds and blazes are, and which lies between the mounds and the Lusnia, and likewise the entire Lusnia woodland, are manifestly shown to be Trąbczyn's — so no Biskupice ground can reach here, nor can there be any Biskupice corner mound or convergence. And it therefore follows that the judicial site inspection of 1589 — conducted in the field and attested in the Konin [municipal court] in 1592 — upon which the Honourable Chełmski bases his entire boundary line and corner mounds, is contrary not only to written documents

but also to those on the ground, and to the position of places. The corner mounds — both this one and the first at the head of the Ostrowce woodland — and the whole general boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski are wrong and unjust. And likewise the claim to the empty Trąbczyn stawisko, under the pretext of the pond which the Honourable Chełmski still has on Łukomia — called the new pond — is unjust and unwarranted.

And so the stawisko and the other woodlands — Ostrowce, Miąsykąt, Dziadulina — and likewise the olędry, meadows, and fields have been wrongly taken from Trąbczyn by the boundary line and corner mounds of the Honourable Chełmski, contrary to all counter-proofs and proofs formed and run; and likewise the corner mounds — the first at the Ostrowce woodland and the Osiny road, acknowledged by the heir of Biskupice by prearranged arrangement — are acknowledged unjustly and dishonestly. Concerning all this and in respect of all this — and concerning the unjust and unlawful acknowledgement of corner mounds to him, both at the starting point and at the terminal point — and likewise concerning the invalidity of the corner mounds and their acknowledgement, and of the entire boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski as run, and of the judicial site inspection of 1592 as attested — as contrary to all proofs, law, and justice, and to the terminality of the law — against the Honourable Chełmski, Gałecki, Starost of Bydgoszcz, heir of Biskupice, and likewise against the Ląd Convent, heirs of Drzewce — unjustly acknowledging corner mounds also contrary to their former acknowledgements and documents to the Honourable Chełmski — the Honourable Prusimski protests and complains most solemnly before his court.

After the conclusion of the review of the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski, the Honourable Prusimski, having returned to Studzienka and the hills and bogs called — as they say — the tavern [hills and bogs] described above, and to the corner mound established there, and wishing further to run the second Trąbczyn boundary with Biskupice from the corner mound described above — he requested his court for execution the boundary line in this boundary. The court following began that boundary line in this boundary from the said Studzienka and the corner mound formed there, and going left straight east toward Czarny Bród — went above the tavern hills through the boggy marsh not far from and opposite Studzienka, lying under the hills, overgrown with clumps and various trees. Going through that bog or marshland for about three staj and turning left from the hills, it came out a little onto a hilly and sparse pine. Then further straight east for about two staj, through the very low ground or small drainage strips sparsely overgrown with birch and other trees, it came to the road from the Trąbczyn olędry to the pine forest; and having crossed that road, came again to a not-very-wide bog sparsely overgrown with various trees. Going beside the Cygany mounds lying some distance to the left of the boundary line, it came out onto bare ground of pine forest. From there, going through the bare ground or sparse pine, alongside and beside the eight Cygany mounds and the Lusnia woodland lying a little closer to the boundary line on the left — also eastward a considerable distance through pure pine — it came to the road from Łukomia or Łomowo to Królikowo, and having crossed that road,

went further straight east toward Czarny Bród through pine likewise for about three staj, beside the blazes shown in the review lying close to the boundary line, and came to the beaten road from Szetlewek to Szymanowice to Jarocin. Entering that road and going several staj east toward Czarny Bród through pine, it came to the cross road from Łazy and the Trąbczyn olędry to Gąsiennice and the Otnie bogs — crossing it: first time through the dark-coloured/boggy [czerniawy] drainage strips and pine forests so called; second time further past the road from Łukomia and Łomowo to Królikowo; third time again past the road from Królikowo and Biskupice to Toporów and Gizałki to Tomice, running past Otnie and Gąsiennice. Having crossed that road, it went also east toward Czarny Bród along the same beaten Jarocin road, above the dark-coloured/boggy drainage strip lying on the right side of the Jarocin road, and came to the road from Łazy and the olędry to Królikowo. Having crossed that road, went further also east along the beaten Jarocin road a considerable distance toward Czarny Bród and came to the large mound called Tomal, lying to the left of the road one staj from Czarny Bród — which mound the people hold to be the corner mound of three estates: Łazy, Trąbczyn Wielki, and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce. Beside that mound stand two large pines with crosses cut into them, and a third also with a cross has been felled.

Having stood at that mound, the Honourable Prusimski determined his boundary line and said that here is the confluence and likewise the corner mound — one for the three above-mentioned estates: Trąbczyn Wielki or the deserted [lands] Drzęzgi, Łazy, and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce. From that mound, beginning again his boundary between Trąbczyn Wielki or the deserted [lands] Drzęzgi and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce, he went further along that same beaten Jarocin road east one staj, and came about one staj to the very bank of Czarny Bród — where the court, unable to conclude this boundary due to excessively high water, proceeded with further actions on the boundary lines already run.

After the running of the boundary lines and the reviews described above, having concluded them — the present commission court, proceeding to the decision of this cause, and resolving its suspension concerning the unsealing and reading of the sworn inquiries submitted by the Honourable Prusimski — proceeded to unseal and read those sworn inquiries. Having unsealed and read them, since many new circumstances now arise both from the boundary lines and the reviews, and moreover since the Ląd Convent, not appearing here in its court and being treated in default, does not submit its sworn inquiries concerning the corner mounds shown at past sessions, and likewise the Honourable Chełmski does not submit his sworn inquiries — the court, wishing to have the most thorough possible knowledge of this cause and endeavouring to ascertain it in all respects, considers a supplementary sworn inquiry to be necessary.

It orders that the Honourable Prusimski, in proof and counter-proof of his boundary line and that of the Honourable Chełmski, and likewise of the review and the corner mounds and other places in the boundary lines and reviews of both parties shown to his court — draw up and sign interrogatories as thoroughly as possible and submit them to his court for the hearing of knowledgeable, credible, and unexceptionable witnesses — and, taking a preceding oath of non-subornation upon them, introduce those witnesses into his court. The Honourable Prusimski, complying with his court's order, drew up, signed, and submitted the interrogatories to his court. Upon receiving them, his court orders the Honourable Prusimski to take an oath in this formula:

"That the witnesses whom I shall introduce for this sworn inquiry I have neither suborned nor bribed — by anyone or by myself — nor have I engaged them in any other improper manner to the detriment of my neighbour, and I shall not suborn, bribe, or engage them — so help me God and the innocent Passion of His Son."

For the proclamation of that oath,

the Court Summoner assisting this act is assigned. The Honourable Prusimski performed that oath in the formula described above and introduced the witnesses. Thereupon the present commission court, having proceeded to hear the sworn inquiry and having first from each witness individually heard the oath for truthful testimony, conducted those sworn inquiries in the customary manner. After hearing them, and having heard the further controversies of the parties introduced into its court, it proceeded to the resolution and decision of this cause — from the sworn inquiries: two older and the present third, conducted. Having read those sworn inquiries and confronted them with the documents and with the position and layout of places subjected to the required visual inspection as carefully, attentively, and diligently as possible — it proceeded first, as to the resolution of the corner mound at the starting point — as established and formed by both the Honourable Prusimski and the Honourable Chełmski and by the Ląd Convent at several past sessions and now — as ordered to be resolved first by the Tribunal decree.

Since, both from the sworn inquiries read — three in all — and from what was observed on the ground as is customary in a field cause, and likewise from the documents expressed in the schedule by the Honourable Prusimski and signed by the President of his court, and likewise noted in the present act alongside the controversies — it is apparent that although the Honourable Chełmski takes as his greatest foundation for the present cause the judicial site inspection of the late Wojciech Trąbczyński — supposedly heir of all of Trąbczyn — conducted pursuant to the four Konin land decrees between the same Wojciech Trąbczyński as plaintiff on the one side and the late Honourables Tomasz, Jan, and Stanisław Łukomski and Barbara Brodzińska, widow of the late Sebastian Modliboski, co-heirs of Łukomia, summoned separately on the other, of 1589

on Monday before the Feast of Saint Vitus, Martyr — concerning the flooding of the grounds, pine forests, woodlands, meadows, and pastures on the estate of Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały and Osiny by a new pond built on the Łukomia estate — conducted in the field in the same year 1589, on Monday on the vigil of Saint James the Apostle, and attested three years later, in 1592, on Thursday after the Feast of the Immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin Mary, in the Konin municipal court — yet to the contrary it is proved to his court by a default decree (default, or the penalty of default) between the same Wojciech, uncle and guardian of his below-named nephews, Wawrzyniec, Walenty, Rachwall, and Stanisław Trąbczyński, co-heirs of Trąbczyn inseparably with him, as plaintiffs on the one side and the Ląd Convent on the other — concerning the flooding of the Trąbczyn meadows by the Tarszew mill, of 1584, on Monday before the Feast of Saint Luke, in the Konin land court — and likewise by three subsequent decrees of 1589 on Monday before the Feast of Saint Vitus in the same Konin land court: namely two, first between the same late Honourable Wojciech, uncle and guardian, and his above-mentioned nephews together and inseparably as plaintiffs on the one side and the late Honourable Mikołaj Trąbczyński, heir of a portion of Szetlew, as defendant on the other, concerning the digging of a ditch and the ploughing up of a road; and a third between those Trąbczyński, uncle and guardian, and his nephews together as plaintiffs and the Ląd Convent, concerning the flooding of the Trąbczyn meadows by the Tarszew mill — namely: that Wojciech Trąbczyński, being not himself sole heir of all of Trąbczyn

or the whole Trąbczyński estate, but together with his above-mentioned nephews, and moreover being their guardian — this judicial site inspection, as expressed above, conducted on the vigil of Saint James in 1589, or in truth only from his own portion, yet to the detriment of his nephews who were minors, contrary to the express law enacted on boundary causes, was made and conducted. Nevertheless he did not have it attested in the municipal court in the same year; but when thereafter in 1591 — as attested by the decree of that year, on Monday after the Sunday Jubilate, in the Konin land court, between his nephews on the one side and the same Wojciech Trąbczyński on the other — he was summoned by his nephews concerning the allocation of the portion of Trąbczyn belonging to them after their father, and the further proceedings on the unconcluded decree or partition continued between them — only then, standing in dispute and lawsuit with his nephews for a year after the decree and three years after the judicial site inspection was conducted in the field — namely in 1592, on Tuesday after the Feast of the Immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin Mary, in the Konin municipal court — expressly to the detriment of his nephews — he had it attested.

And moreover it is proved to his court by documents issued that even after this judicial site inspection the further proceedings concerning that partition between those parties continued and were pursued, and those parties remained in dispute and lawsuit — and so both Wojciech Trąbczyński and his nephews were heirs of Trąbczyn each from their portions. And moreover he proves this by the decree of acquittal of 1593 on Monday before

Saint Hedwig — between the same Wojciech, uncle and guardian, and his above-mentioned nephews together as plaintiffs on the one side and the Ląd Convent on the other, concerning the flooding of the Trąbczyn meadows by the Tarszew mill, in the same Konin land court — proving that Wojciech Trąbczyński even after that decree continued to hold his nephews in guardianship and was their guardian and co-heir. And moreover it is adduced to his court by the decree of 1595 on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi in the same Konin land court, between the same Wojciech Trąbczyński and Tomasz Łukomski — that Wojciech Trąbczyński, manifestly acting to the detriment of his nephews and in effect in league with the heirs of Łukomia, limited for six years by the Konin land court decree the oath ordered upon the heir of Łukomia — as to how the newly built pond and the holding back of water had caused no flooding and no damage in the grounds, pine forests, and woodlands of Trąbczyn, Nowa Wieś, and Osiny. And moreover he adduces to his court a terminus from the side of the heirs of Trąbczyn — namely Stanisław from Wojciech, Stanisław from Jan, Andrzej from Rachwall, Stanisław from Mikołaj Trąbczyński — against the Ląd Convent, concerning the demarcation of Kopojno, issued in 1616, extracted from the Konin land court records — proving that even after that judicial site inspection and in that year there were several heirs of Trąbczyn.

And finally it is still further proved to his court by the partition of the sixth October 1693 in Trąbczyn, between Andrzej, Jan, and Władysław Trąbczyński, concerning the division, extracted from the Pyzdry municipal court — that in that year too there was divided ownership in Trąbczyn. And that this divided ownership only then — as attested by the

accounting and the contract between the late Honourable Jan on Kolno Prusimski on the one side and the late Honourables Anna Bojanowska, of her first marriage Trąbczyńska and thereafter Golińska, widow and mother, with Jan and Andrzej, her sons Trąbczyński, in her own name and in the name of her brother Władysław, of 1698, concerning the purchase of those estates, attested in the Poznań municipal court on Monday after Saint Margaret, under pledge of ninety thousand Polish złoty — passed to single ownership. And thereupon, referring to the sworn inquiry and confronting that judicial site inspection together with the sworn inquiries and with other further documents, and likewise with the position of places and from what was observed on the ground — since that judicial site inspection, although conducted in such manner, not by the heir of all but only of a certain portion of Trąbczyn, Wojciech Trąbczyński, and moreover by the guardian of his nephews, then minors in his guardianship and in lawsuit with him at the time of the attestation of this judicial site inspection — past them, to their detriment and expressly by prior arrangement with the heirs of Łukomia, previously arranged, as expressed above — and that privately, by a Court Summoner and nobleman, not by a Boundary Surveyor as was customary at that time, and not by the defendant party but by the plaintiff —

and one might say without any decree at all, since it was not ordered for the plaintiff — is shown not only to be contrary to the Crown statute on minors and on boundary causes, and to the sworn inquiry and to the very corner mounds of the Honourable Chełmski at the starting point and at the terminal point — in short to his entire boundary line, as will be expressed below — but also because the position of the corner mounds of the three estates of Łukom, Trąbczyn, and Drzewce bordering in a corner is shown from nowhere else but from the road which runs to Pyzdry between the estates

of Trąbczyn, Osiny, Łukomia, and the Ląd Convent estate — which words "from the road" can and should rightly be taken and interpreted as nothing other than "before" or "not far from the road", and "between the estates" as nothing other than "between the villages or boundaries". And that road, in the place where the Honourable Chełmski establishes his corner mound supposedly pursuant to that same judicial site inspection — at the head of the Ostrowce woodland — is shown from the sworn inquiry and likewise from what was observed on the ground to be the road that comes from the very village of Osiny and stretches all the way to the beaten Zagórów road, past the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski, and joins it there — and then crosses through the Zagórów road below the hill and runs to Łukom toward Pyzdry. It is the Osiny road and has been so called from of old; it is not the old Pyzdry road, nor does it go between the estates of Trąbczyn, Osiny, Łukomia, and the Ląd Convent estate — that is Drzewce, Zagórów, and Wrąbczyn, lying beside Łukomia on the right side — where it runs above the Ostrowce woodland [i.e., to the north of the Ostrowce woodland] shown from the sworn inquiry, and above the thirteen Drzewce-Trąbczyn wall mounds [i.e., to the north of them], past the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski and into the Zagórów road — and then crosses it below the hill and only then runs to Łukom toward Pyzdry. And which even now they call not the Pyzdry road but the Osiny road. And even the Honourable Chełmski himself at three field sessions — as attested by three field decrees: one of 1759 and two of 1760, expressed in the boundary line alongside the controversies from the Oblata records — so called it and so described and expressed it in those decrees and in the protests lodged by the parties at those decrees.

And moreover: that road is not before the corner mounds, nor from nor toward the mounds, but lies between the two corner mounds themselves. And the corner mounds are not from the road, as the judicial site inspection states, but are formed on both sides of the road — contrary to the judicial site inspection.

And moreover it is shown from the sworn inquiry, and from the judicial site inspection of 1644 in the Pyzdry municipal court on Monday post

Dominicam rogationum — conducted by the heirs of Trąbczyn against the heirs of Łukomia in the Ostrowce woodland — and likewise from two default decrees in the same year 1644 in the Konin land court on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi, and from two decrees in the Konin court on Monday after Saint Hedwig in the same year 1644, concerning the felling of timber on poles for nets in that woodland, with the Łukomia heirs, with motions to the Tribunal admitted — and likewise from two Piotrków Tribunal decrees of deferral in the same matter, of 1645 on Tuesday before Saint Prisca, from the motions in both cases — that this woodland, at the head or beginning of which the Honourable Chełmski establishes his corner mound, has from of old been called Ostrowce and is proper Trąbczyn property and has always been used and enclosed to Trąbczyn, and the Łukomia estate and its pine forest does not reach here.

Only here is the ground and pine forest — that is the Ostrowce woodland — belonging to Trąbczyn, to the left westward from here; and to the right to Drzewce up to the very mounds — and has been so used from of old both by Trąbczyn and by Drzewce. And those two mounds — #14 and #15 — which the Honourable Chełmski takes as corner mounds, are not corner mounds but are, together with the thirteen stretching from here to the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski — which Chełmski formerly took and claimed as wall mounds supposedly in a wall between Drzewce and Łukomia — wall mounds between Drzewce and Trąbczyn. And further mounds of the same form are

more numerous in that same wall on the other side of those two mounds, all the way to the Kopojno corner mound. Conversely, from those same sworn inquiries and documents, and likewise from what was observed on the ground, it is shown that the road which the Honourable Prusimski showed in his boundary line actually lies before the three corner mounds — and the mounds lie about one staj from it — is the old Pyzdry road, sufficiently visible and in one place deeply worn above the Zagórów road. And that this road actually runs between the estates or villages of Trąbczyn Mały, Osiny, Drzewce, and further Łukomia and other villages or estates of the Ląd Convent — that is Zagórów, Wrąbczyn, and the ground in them — and likewise past Łukomia (which lies on this side from Zagórów and Drzewce, and has its wall on this side, above the old Pyzdry road, as demarcated by the compromise decree of 1761) — and not toward Łukom as the current Osiny road goes. That road did go there; and nevertheless even now the present road to Pyzdry joins that same old Pyzdry road beyond Łukomia — which cannot be avoided on account of the marshes — and so, joining the old Pyzdry road, reaches Pyzdry itself. This present Osiny road to Pyzdry does not and cannot go between the above-named estates and their grounds, since neither the Drzewce ground nor the Zagórów ground reaches through the Łukomia ground — which lies between both those roads, old and new, taking from the Zagórów road — to this present Osiny road, given that ground and its wall, and cannot reach it; but starting only from the Zagórów road, it runs through the Łukomia ground itself even contrary to the judicial site inspection. And therefore the old Pyzdry road — and likewise the corner mounds, as the judicial site inspection of 1592 states, agreeing only as to this place and showing the position of the corner mound, but not being able to determine boundaries —

are shown here

and proved. And likewise the Zagórów road through Drzewce to Tomice, Kalisz, and Chocz — running beside the corner mounds and called the Zagórów or Drzewce road — and from it the road toward Łukomia branching to the right — are also present and actually found there. And moreover it is shown both from the sworn inquiry and the documents that here in this place at those three corner mounds the ground and pine forest of three estates converge: that of Łukomia, or [as] separated to the Ląd Convent by the compromise decree — to the west, on the right side of the Zagórów or Drzewce road; on the left side of that same Zagórów road and the corner mounds — the Ostrowce woodland, touching on one side here the mounds and on the other all the way to the stawisko further described in the boundary — to the south; and the ground and pine forest of the third estate, Drzewce — to the north. And so they converge from of old, used thus by all those estates.

And there is no separate corner mound or convergence of Bukowe here — which Chełmski at three past sessions said and established was a separate corner mound or convergence here and rejected Trąbczyn — as attested by the field decree of 1760, made in the field between the Ląd Convent and the Honourable Chełmski and filed in the Konin municipal court in 1761 on Monday after the Sunday Jubilate: that it has not been demarcated from time immemorial and is only an estate ground of Łukomia lying within the ground and body of the Łukomia boundaries; it does not exist separately but together with Łukomia — and Bukowe is the same as Łukom. Therefore the convergence here is of Łukomia or Bukowe — and this is attested not only by the present sworn inquiry and the above-mentioned field decree, but also by two further decrees:

one in default or the penalty of default of 1644 on Monday after the Octave of Corpus Christi, and a second from serious controversies with a motion to the Tribunal admitted on Monday after Saint Hedwig in the same year — between the heirs of Trąbczyn and [those of] Łukomia, concerning the demarcation of those

estates — setting forth that besides Bukowe, Łukom, Łomowo, and Imielno are and lie in one ground. And moreover that the road which comes from Zagórów through Drzewce is called both the Zagórów and the Drzewce road, and from it a road to Łukomia also branches — all of which is acknowledged not only by the sworn inquiry but also by the serfs of Drzewce named above: namely the Diligent Tomasz and Kazimierz Czerzniakowie, Tomasz and Fabian, Stefan Głąbik the alderman, Jan Kowal, and Kazimierz Dąbek the cartwright. And finally their very lords — the Ląd Convent — at three sessions conducted from Tribunal decrees, established and acknowledged that same corner mound and mounds and confluence of three estates — Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn — not admitting Bukowe but on the contrary rejecting it — bordering their Drzewce wall with Łukomia in that same place, as attested by the above-mentioned field decrees: one of 1759 and two of 1760. And likewise the Honourable Prusimski, heir of Trąbczyn, at that time appearing as a collateral neighbour as to their boundary line, acknowledged and asserted the same as he does now. And moreover the Honourable Chełmski himself acknowledged and said that same corner mound and mounds in this place at all three sessions — with only the difference that he placed Bukowe instead of Trąbczyn here and rejected Trąbczyn. But thereafter, having demarcated in 1761 through a compromise with the Ląd Convent and having abandoned his assertion of Bukowe, he began and demarcated the Łukomia wall with Drzewce instead — and determined it from the Zagórów corner mound, straight opposite and facing the above-described corner mound shown by the Honourable Prusimski, at only one staj — and heaped the last mound in that wall, as attested by the compromise decree of 1761 between him and the Ląd Convent, filed in the Kalisz municipal court on Monday after the Sunday Cantate.

And there at that mound, leaving with his Łukomia wall — which must necessarily

determine at the corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski — only the further Drzewce wall to be taken, which can only run with Trąbczyn and no other estate — he designated this by that same decree, and thereby legitimised and confirmed that same corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski and put aside his assertion of Bukowe. And moreover that corner mound and convergence of the above-mentioned estates — Łukomia, Trąbczyn, and Drzewce — not only the said decrees, not only the sworn inquiry, not only the repeated acknowledgements at past sessions of the Ląd Convent and now of the Drzewce people and of the Honourable Prusimski, and finally of the Honourable Chełmski himself — with only the difference of Bukowe — but also old documents confirm and corroborate [that it lies] nowhere else but at the confluence with Drzewce, and not in that other place where the Honourable Chełmski asserts it to be, but in this very place where the Honourable Prusimski shows it to be and where it ought to be — namely two decrees: one default or the penalty of default, and a second from controversies, against the heirs of Łukomia, principally summoned for the demarcation of Łukomia with Trąbczyn; and two similar ones with Drzewce, concerning the showing of a corner mound and likewise demarcation with them — in the Konin land court of 1644, expressed in the boundary line from the acta et oblata.

Therefore the present commission court, proceeding first to the resolution of the said corner mounds, finds the corner mound of the Honourable Chełmski to be wrong and improper — contrary to all documents and sworn inquiries and even to the very judicial site inspection expressed above — formed on proper Trąbczyn ground at the head or beginning of the woodland called Ostrowce, proved in this place, from two mounds, namely #14 and #15 — wall mounds of Drzewce and likewise of Trąbczyn — and from a third hillock of a stump, and that not as a corner

but actually as a wall mound, and not at the confluence of three estates (since Łukom does not reach there). And at the same time the acknowledgement of this corner mound by the Ląd Convent — made inconsistently and contrary to its former acknowledgements — it finds and holds to be unjust and unwarranted, repudiates, and overturns it.

The corner mound of the Honourable Prusimski, however — as lying at three mounds actually present here in this place, and lying not far from the old Pyzdry road proved here, and being the actual confluence, as a corner, of three estates, namely Łukomia, Drzewce, and Trąbczyn — as visibly shown in conformity with the sworn inquiries, documents, and the position of places and villages — and acknowledged at three sessions by the Ląd Convent and now by the Drzewce people and likewise by the Honourable Prusimski, and finally by the Honourable Chełmski himself — with only the difference of Bukowe — it adjudges, finds, and approves to be good and proper.

[Section 6: Court's Resolution on the Full Boundary Line — Mound-Heaping, Trąbczyn–Łukomia — Oath]

As to the further boundary line of both parties, the route through and the terminal point — since it is shown both from the sworn inquiries and from the documents that the Ostrowce woodland, lying as described above on one side from the corner mounds of the Honourable Prusimski and the fourteen Drzewce-Trąbczyn wall mounds, with the road between those wall mounds and the stawisko running in breadth to the south, and beginning from the supposed corner mound of the Honourable Chełmski already resolved by the court, and stretching in length westward as far as the Zagórów road — is, as attested by the judicial site inspection and proceeding submitted above, proper Trąbczyn property and has been used from of old to Trąbczyn —

and likewise the two mounds going from the corner mounds toward the stawisko — one not far from the corner mounds near the old wolf pit, deep and the other below the hill beside the Osiny — now Pyzdry — road, described in the boundary line, are actual and genuine mounds — and the second mound below the hill, although not so prominent as the first

from the corner mounds, some witnesses reach to it by their memory and observation that it was formerly larger — and that the further boundary of those estates runs along the Zagórów or Kalisz road, which runs beside the corner mounds and beside those two mounds, between the Ostrowce woodland and the pine forest together with the woodland lying to the left of the road in this place, and the Łukomia or convent-separated pine forest lying to the right of the road — running south toward the stawisko and thereafter further beside the stawisko — as acknowledged and asserted by the Honourable Chełmski himself when allotting a portion of pine forest to the Łukomia — to the Ląd Convent for three łany of land by the compromise decree of 1761, made between him and the Ląd Convent, filed in the Kalisz municipal court in the same year on Monday after the Sunday Cantate, and a mound heaped beside that same road as a marker of the pine forest separated — the sworn inquiries and documents attest that it is actual and a boundary road.

And moreover it is shown from the two Trąbczyn partitions — one of 1522 between the Honourables Wojciech and Stanisław, brothers by birth Trąbczyński, heirs of the whole Trąbczyński estate, attested in the Konin municipal court on Thursday in the Octave of the Ascension of the Lord — that the pond with meadows at Nowa Wieś fell to Stanisław Trąbczyński; and that no pond with or without meadows other than this present stawisko, as is apparent from the partition below and from the review of the entire ground Nowej Wsi made for his court, exists at Nowa Wieś. And a second

of the sixth October 1693, between Andrzej, Jan, and Władysław Trąbczyński, heirs of Trąbczyn with appurtenances, dated and made in Trąbczyn and extracted from the Pyzdry municipal court — by which the Trąbczyński, dividing the estates, reserve to themselves the remaining ponds or rather empty marshes for common fishing, and likewise the flat marshes overgrown with clumps for common use, and likewise that meadows which serfs have long mown should not be taken from them — prove that this stawisko, or rather the empty marshes from that pond depleted over time, belonged from of old to Trąbczyn and Trąbczyn used them, and the serfs — especially from Nowa Wieś and Osiny — had meadows on that stawisko and mowed them; and the grandfather or great-grandfather of the Honourable Chełmski (who once came upon a serf mowing right below the dam itself) did not forbid it and said nothing to him — and indeed, having spoken with him there, rode away. And moreover the four decrees of 1589 expressed above attest this, and the oath ordered by the Konin land court upon the late Tomasz Łukomski, heir of Łukomia — limited for six years in 1595 by the Honourable Wojciech Trąbczyński and thereafter not prosecuted — that the flooding was made and caused on the Trąbczyn Wielki and Mały and Osiny estate by the pond newly built with a dam below the stawisko [i.e., to the west of the stawisko] toward Łukomia on the Łukomia estate by the heirs of Łukomia — and that that flooding was nowhere else but on that stawisko and on those empty marshes — especially since not a little timber, both standing and fallen and dried out, is present on that stawisko.

And moreover that the stawisko or marshes stretch for nearly a mile past Osiny all the way to Czarny Bród and further

to the Królikowo marshes and ponds — and throughout that stretch, and likewise in the place where the Honourable Chełmski ran his boundary line through that stawisko, there is no watershed. And moreover, on the side of that stawisko beyond the Miąsykąt woodland, there are other marshes which are called the duck bogs — and these too, as the sworn inquiry plainly sets forth, were used to Trąbczyn and Łukom did not use or forbid them. And moreover the Honourable Chełmski has below that stawisko [i.e., to the west of it] toward Łukomia a pond — sufficiently large and long — worked on those same marshes on the other side of the Kalisz road, likewise in pine, and, as it is overgrown with clumps and various trees and has only a mill race cut before the mill below the dam, on which pond is a mill and the dam is well made, two staj long, high, and wide — and that pond, having been newly contrived on the marshes, is called the new pond. And it is by that pond and dam, and by holding the water so high, that the flooding of that same stawisko — and of the meadows, pastures, and timber upon it — was caused. Under the pretext of that pond — which the Łukomia heir still has to this day — the heirs of Łukomia, that is the father or grandfather of the Honourable Chełmski, began to encroach upon the said stawisko; and so on it first built a dam of clay and then of timber in the manner of a small weir or sluice in the very middle of the dam — holding back the water so that too much should not go wastefully to his mill — and thereafter encroached also for the meadows. But the heirs of Trąbczyn, that is the predecessors of the Honourable Prusimski — as attested by the numerous judicial site inspections and protests and the decree of 1728 and other acts — resisted and disputed this.

And moreover

it is shown that the Miąsykąt, Dziadulina, and Lusnia woodlands, stretching one after another from that stawisko toward Czarny Bród, always belonged to Trąbczyn and were in use — and as they were proper Trąbczyn property, the uncle of the Honourable Prusimski established olędry upon those woodlands in the time of the father of the Honourable Chełmski — as attested by the rights granted them in 1746 — and the predecessor of the Honourable Chełmski neither resisted nor objected to that settlement, which he would not have omitted had he known those woodlands to be his; or at the very least he would at that time have lodged some manifest or judicial site inspection — none of which the Honourable Chełmski shows in the course of this cause. And moreover it is still shown from those sworn inquiries that the mound immediately beside the pond, above the Zagórów or Kalisz road — after crossing the ditch running from the stawisko toward the Honourable Chełmski's pond — was a sufficiently prominent wall mound which the people saw and remember; and that mound — as the judicial site inspection of 1726 attest — proves to have been ploughed up or scattered by the grandfather of the Honourable Chełmski. And moreover the other mounds above the same Kalisz road are shown to have been damaged and scattered by the former heir of Łukomia, for the ruin of the old boundaries. And moreover it is still apparent from those sworn inquiries that the small field about half a staj beyond that pond — to the left of the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski and the Zagórów or Kalisz road — on which rather large pine trees now stand, always belonged to and was enclosed by Trąbczyn — for which reason that small field, as is apparent from the very pine trees, has grown up from old.

And moreover it is shown from those sworn inquiries and documents that the old mound described in the boundary line above the small oak, to the right of the Kalisz road, sufficiently round with a small ditch and crown around it, and likewise the other two

above the same Kalisz road to the other side of the road — to the left — described in the boundary line, are genuine and real mounds; and likewise the blazes on old and large pines to the left of the road — and together with the use of [the road], they confirm that the Kalisz road is a boundary. And moreover the dry ford — which is now dry but is now called the Kalisz ford — and the wooden footboards laid upon it, in the branching of the Zagórów or Kalisz road toward Grądzeń, and likewise that same road — and none other — to Grądzeń running west, expressed in Mieleński's judicial site inspection of 1658 — are shown not only from the sworn inquiry but and from what was observed on the ground. And above all it is shown from that very Mieleński judicial site inspection that when the Ląd Convent, demarcating or conducting the judicial site inspection of its Grądzeń ground officially pursuant to a Piotrków Tribunal decree, came to that Kalisz road and at the same time to the Trąbczyn wall above that road — the heir of Trąbczyn, standing there beside that road by reason of his boundary, held that same Kalisz road to be a boundary, asserted the ground and pine forest to the left of that road as his Trąbczyn property and that to the right as Grądzeń's — and the Convent acknowledged the same to him.

Yet the predecessor of the Honourable Chełmski, heir of Łukomia, protested against him at that judicial site inspection — not that he was acknowledging the Kalisz road as a boundary, but only that he was attributing the wall not to Łukomia but to Grądzeń. By which that

predecessor of the Honourable Chełmski, having at that time better knowledge than now, acknowledged the Kalisz road and did not deny it. As to the further boundary line in this boundary above the duck bogs [i.e., to the north of the duck bogs] — as expressed above, these belong and belonged to Trąbczyn and Trąbczyn used them up to the time of the delivery of possession. And moreover the forked pine is stated to stand on an actual mound and the mound under it is to be found there. And moreover the sworn inquiries assert and show the further boundary line in that boundary and its continuity — and weighing the continuity — since not only the sworn inquiries but also the documents expressed above in the boundary line, namely the proceedings and decrees and the oath with the heirs of Biskupice concerning the private nocturnal heaping of eight mounds and the making of forty-one blazes on trees, attest that the eight mounds called Cygany are heaped privately and by night on Trąbczyn ground, at no small expense and detriment to the Trąbczyn ground — but the mounds, taking from the first, lying about one and a half staj from the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski and the Kalisz road beside it, and the Lusnia woodland enclose and show still more of the further boundary line and continuity of the Trąbczyn boundary and ground, and repel the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski.

And finally, since the corner mound or confluence of the three estates — Trąbczyn, Łukomia, and Biskupice — and the determination of the terminal point as to this boundary, at Studzienka — and moreover that Studzienka itself, faced with brick and demolished by the Honourable Chełmski so that people going on pilgrimage to Zagórów, not finding water in that well, might resort to his inn — which nonetheless still exists, though without facingwork — and likewise the cross on the pine opposite that Studzienka, above the very road, and likewise three corner mounds said to be there,

are shown to be there; and finally that the tavern hills and bogs lying there and subject to the court's inspection, holding their name from the inn once in the pine nearby belonging to Królikowo or Białły — the ruins of which also were shown to the court — are shown not only by the sworn inquiry but also by Mieleński's judicial site inspection of 1658. And moreover the blazes on various pines above the Kalisz road and the use of the pine forest up to this point — and further, as some witnesses assert — are maintained by Trąbczyn and proved and shown.

Since from those sworn inquiries and the above-mentioned documents it is shown that the Honourable Chełmski first established a corner mound on Trąbczyn ground, and then beginning from his corner mound — already resolved and adjudged by its court — took in his boundary line the entire Ostrowce woodland, and half the stawisko or rather empty marshes, and the Miąsykąt, Dziadulina, and Lusnia woodlands, and nearly all the fields and meadows of the olędry, and the twelve olędry themselves and the inn — belonging to Trąbczyn from of old as has been shown above — and used by them. And moreover a few years before the unlawful and improper delivery of possession, in 1771, he took possession of them — and that whole boundary line, as at the starting point improperly begun, so through and to the terminal point run through the proper grounds, fields, and woodlands of Trąbczyn. And he took half the stawisko, larger, supposedly to a watershed — of which there is none there and is not apparent and cannot be — since the stawisko or marshes extend uniformly for a mile in all directions. And the Miąsykąt woodlands — of which

there is no mention whatsoever in the judicial site inspection of 1589 made in the field and attested in 1592 — he took on some unknown foundation. And moreover the hillocks which he showed in his boundary line as mounds are shown from the sworn inquiry and likewise from what was observed on the ground during the review of his boundary line — and from the judicial site inspection of this year conducted in the boundary line — attested in the Konin municipal court on Tuesday before the Feast of Saint Francis by the Court Summoner and nobleman — that the mounds shown and named as such in the boundary and at the corner mound formed at the Lusnia woodland are not mounds but hillocks or stumps, and many more of the same kind and in great number are found in the pine forest. And finally, since both the sworn inquiry and the proceedings and decrees with the heirs of Biskupice — both in the Kalisz land court at the Konin sessions and in the Tribunal — concerning the heaping of eight mounds and the making of forty-one blazes above the Lusnia, and the swearing-off ordered upon Biskupski with two witnesses of equal birth, performed by him — expressed above in the controversy at the review — show that those eight mounds and blazes are on Trąbczyn ground, and with great expense and detriment to the Trąbczyn ground above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of the Lusnia woodland] beginning from the corduroy crossings, privately and by night — and for that reason they are called Cygany.

And those mounds and blazes are actually — as is apparent from the sworn inquiry and the court's own review — above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of it], about

one staj from the woodland, and stretch toward the corduroy crossings — which corduroy crossings, as attested by the sworn inquiry, lie not far from Czarny Bród — and moreover they enclose the corner mound of the Honourable Chełmski and extend still further toward the corduroy crossings; and the heir of Biskupice swore off those mounds and blazes, saying that he had not heaped nor made them — and so it is apparent from this that he had not usurped to himself the ground enclosed by those mounds and blazes — extending more than one staj from the Lusnia woodland in breadth toward the mounds — and likewise the Lusnia woodland; and even if he had usurped, and through that usurpation had made those mounds and blazes, yet having sworn off those mounds and blazes and renounced their effects, he has already renounced and sworn off both the ground on which those mounds and blazes stand and that which lies between the mounds and the Lusnia woodland.

And it follows from this that he has already acknowledged and shown that ground to be Trąbczyn's; and since those mounds and blazes were made at great expense and detriment to the Trąbczyn ground, therefore they show and prove not only the title and ownership of that ground on which they stand, and of that which lies between them and the Lusnia woodland, to belong to Trąbczyn, but also the entirety of the Lusnia woodland and moreover the further continuity and breadth of the Trąbczyn ground — which the mounds and blazes themselves, or rather their actual and obvious existence, clinch and demonstrate — and by the same token do not permit Biskupice to reach the Lusnia woodland and to the alleged corner mound of the Honourable Chełmski, nor any convergence with Łukomia and Trąbczyn at the Lusnia

woodland; and on the contrary, they assert and maintain that the ground and pine forest lying between those Cygany mounds and blazes and the Lusnia woodland — from the corduroy crossings all the way to the Kalisz road, and to the Kalisz road itself (since, taking from the corduroy crossings, the last Cygany mound lies near the Kalisz road, as is apparent from the first review) — must extend and reach to Trąbczyn. And that this is so is proved and demonstrated: that the ground lying between the Lusnia woodland and the mounds and blazes, together with the entire Lusnia woodland, and likewise that on which the mounds and blazes stand — and still further, in breadth and in length beyond the mounds — has without question been used; and it is thus openly and demonstrably apparent that neither the Honourable Chełmski nor the heir of Biskupice can or should have their convergence here in this place above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of it] as they claim, pursuant to the judicial site inspection mentioned so many times.

For if the corner mound and convergence of the Biskupice estates were to be found here in this place, then all the ground and pine forest lying between the Lusnia woodland and the Cygany mounds and blazes, and likewise that on which the Cygany mounds and blazes stand — and still further in breadth to the south and in length to the east, belonging to Trąbczyn — on one side from the corner mound, east toward the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród, the heir of Biskupice, and on the other side from that same corner mound, west toward the Kalisz road, the heir of Łukomia — together with the Cygany mounds and blazes, in Trąbczyn's own proper ground as proved by sworn inquiry and documents — and still further, a large portion of the Trąbczyn ground and

pine forest in breadth and in length — both would take away, and both would divide between themselves another's Trąbczyn ground, and between themselves would divide the boundary of the Honourable Prusimski confirmed by so many proofs — which is shown and proved to his court openly and actually not only from the sworn inquiry, not only from the documents, the arguments, and the party's exposition, but also from the position and layout of the places and of the Cygany mounds so called and the blazes expressed above, and from what was observed on the ground. And finally, since it appears that the boundary of the Honourable Prusimski is confirmed — by the documents, by the sworn inquiry, by the ownership of the woodlands, and notably Ostrowce, the stawisko, Dziadulina, Miąsykąt, Lusnia — by the use of them, by actual mounds and corner mounds, by the boundary road Kalisz or Zagórów, by Studzienka and the tavern hills and bogs, and by the other markers and blazes shown in the boundary line and other positions of places, and finally by the actual position and existence of the Cygany mounds, and by the settlement of the olędry as so long established and genuine, confirmed by documents and sworn inquiries and by the position and location of places, and finally in agreement with Crown law —

then the Honourable Chełmski, the boundary wall of Trąbczyn standing in the way, legitimately proved and adduced, in no way can — pursuant to the judicial site inspection of 1592 — reach Biskupice, nor have a corner mound here in this place at the Lusnia woodland, nor can they converge there; and otherwise pursuant to that judicial site inspection he can reach Biskupice only necessarily through Trąbczyn's own ground — just as the Honourable Chełmski now does. And that this is so is shown by the later judicial site inspection of 1593,

conducted on Thursday on the very Feast of Saint Mary Magdalene, from the side of the Łukomia heirs, in that those same persons — going, or rather leading the mounds to Biskupice — wishing to accomplish this could not do so except by way of the Osiny boundaries — which are the same as the Trąbczyn boundaries. And accordingly, those and such proofs, sworn inquiries, documents, and finally the Cygany mounds and blazes and all other arguments and circumstances being weighed — since not only the above-mentioned judicial site inspection but also the present boundary between Trąbczyn and Łukomia as run by the Honourable Chełmski, and his corner mound, are shown to be at variance with all those proofs, and this is proved to his court —

therefore the present commission court, proceeding by virtue of the constitution or commission issued by the Estates of the Commonwealth for the full adjudication of the present cause and the weighing of documents — and considering and observing the proofs expressed above, which are without any doubt, and on the contrary demonstrate and confirm the reality of the ancient boundaries and the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski; and likewise the Crown law established in boundary causes, which orders the Sub-chamberlain court to observe and maintain the greater probability in boundaries, and to admit the party demonstrating the better improvement and the more notable markers to the oath upon honour and conscience — and with respect to the said judicial site inspection, as conducted by the heir of only a portion and not the whole of Trąbczyn, during a guardianship held over his nephews who were also co-heirs of Trąbczyn along with him, without them, and on the contrary, a dispute and discord standing

between them — and that only from the portion of one heir, past the other heirs — and with prejudice to them, expressly by prior arrangement with the heirs of Łukomia previously arranged, contrary to the law of nature and the Crown law, and contrary to the proofs expressed above and to the ancient boundaries confirmed by so many documents and mounds as now proved by the Honourable Prusimski, and contrary to the layout of places and villages — conducted; and contrary to all documents issued before and after it expressed above, and to the sworn inquiry, and finally contrary to the Honourable Chełmski's own boundary line and corner mounds; and moreover as ordered to be maintained only insofar as it was not contrary to other documents, by the last decree of the Bydgoszcz Tribunal of 1768; and as being unable to cause prejudice and harm to other heirs, the pledgors and antecessors of the Honourable Prusimski, as not entering into it, and moreover then minors in the guardianship of the said Wojciech Trąbczyński, and — as is apparent from the partition deed of 1693 — remaining in possession of the stawisko or rather of the empty marshes, not secured by that same judicial site inspection; and as unable to establish new boundaries against them — not regarding it,

and on the contrary dismissing and setting aside it as unlawful and irregular, condemned by law itself — it dismisses and sets aside the boundary line of the Honourable Chełmski: both as to the corner mound at the starting point, already resolved, and as to the wall and the terminal point — as run through his own proper ground, pine forests, woodlands, the stawisko or empty marshes, likewise not present in that judicial site inspection — such as Miąsykąt and Dziadulina —

and as run not by way of the watershed of the stawisko but straight through the centre of the stawisko or marshes, contrary to the documents, sworn inquiries, mounds, and other genuine boundary markers expressed in the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski, and contrary to law and ancient use — run contrary and unlawfully, unsupported by any documents or boundary markers — recognises as unjust, unlawful, and inequitable, and rejects and sets it aside entirely — both at the starting point and through and to the terminal point. And the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski, as run and proved in accordance with the sworn inquiries and documents both written and standing on the ground, and confirmed with the survey, and supported by genuine mounds, blazes, and other markers, and by ancient use — together with the corner mound expressed and resolved above, both at the starting point and through and to the terminal point — described already both in the boundary walk and in its sentence above — in all and in everything — it approves, confirms, and adjudges to be the better, and for the greater security thereof, pursuant to the statute's disposition, orders the Honourable Prusimski to swear on the last and final mound heaped above, with six witnesses of equal birth, in the formula prescribed by the statute below, after the mounds have been heaped.

And not having any available — on account of the intervening death of the late Honourable Antoni Wolski, Sub-chamberlain of Inowłocław, and of Bartłomiej Kraszewski, his Inowłocław Boundary Surveyor, appointed pursuant to the constitution of the Commonwealth together with him to this cause — commissioners to heap mounds together from the Sub-chamberlain office, and preserving the rights and the Sub-chamberlain function —

therefore in such circumstances the present commission court, having the power granted it by the Commonwealth for the needs and circumstances of the case in this cause to establish what is necessary and to adjudicate it sufficiently and finally — not regarding also the non-appearance of any party — orders that the Honourable Prusimski, pursuant to the court's judicial declaration issued to him during the writing of the sentence or decree, bring in the Sub-chamberlain office, that is the Kalisz Boundary Surveyor; and that the Boundary Surveyor, once brought in, in the presence of his court shall heap the corner and wall mounds pursuant to the sentence or decree of his court — leaving old mounds and blazes untouched and indeed retaining them for the security and substantive confirmation of his court's judgment — and heaping new ones beside the old ones in the boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski, and shall fully mark out those boundaries with mounds and other legally prescribed markers. After those mounds have been heaped, he shall administer the oath on the last and final mound — together with his court — from the Honourable Prusimski and his six witnesses equal to him in birth, pursuant to the statute formula.

The Honourable Prusimski, complying with his court's order, since he has brought in the Honourable Wojciech Czarnecki, Kalisz Boundary Surveyor — the Honourable Czarnecki, Kalisz Boundary Surveyor, for the execution of his court's decree pursuant to its provisions, proceeded to heap mounds in the boundary line between Trąbczyn and Łukomia, in the presence of his court, or rather together with his court.

And so, beginning the heaping of mounds from the corner mound

or confluence of three estates — Trąbczyn, Łukomia, and Drzewce — and having set out a measuring staff of eight łokci for the measurement of mounds, and having placed the boundary markers — namely several whole bricks, slag, glass, stones, and charcoal — at the places of those mounds, and having applied at the place of the Trąbczyn mound a glass bottle with the description of the commission — he heaped three corner mounds as a corner, one from another in equal measure lying close together beside the old corner mounds left intact just as they were — emerging from their centre, not far from the old Pyzdry road, about one staj before the mounds to the north toward Drzewce, as shown from the sworn inquiry. And describing now more precisely the location of those mounds — namely between three roads: one, the old Pyzdry road, lying above and not far before them; a second, from Zagórów township through Drzewce to Kalisz, Chocz, and Tomice, running beside the mounds — called the Zagórów and also the Drzewce road; and a third, the Osiny road, running from Osiny through the pine forest above the fourteen Drzewce-Trąbczyn wall mounds past the above-described corner mounds and joining the Zagórów road, and then crossing through the Zagórów road below the hill and running now to Łukom toward Pyzdry — which three mounds divide the above-mentioned estates: Łukom to the west, Trąbczyn to the south, Drzewce to the north, and their pine forests.

From those corner mounds, going a little to the right, somewhat past south diagonally — and crossing the Zagórów or Drzewce new road to Kalisz, Chocz, and Tomice — twenty-two pręty along the old Zagórów road: the first wall mound heaped — beside which is the old mound left intact, well prominent, and the wolf pit to the

right of it — where also beside that mound and the wolf pit to the right is a road not very prominent branching from the Zagórów or Drzewce road to the right above, joining the Osiny — now Pyzdry — road by a shortcut below the hill and running together with the Osiny — now Pyzdry — road beside the hill toward Łukomia and to Łukomia. And to the left a pine with a cross blaze newly cut.

Likewise going further along the old road — thirteen pręty — the second wall mound heaped on that same road.
