Likewise going further — the third wall mound, after crossing the new road from Osiny to Pyzdry on the right side of the Zagórów road below the sandy hill — heaped thirteen pręty away. Beside that mound is the old mound, not very prominent but round, shown from the sworn inquiry, and an old pine stump stands upon it; and a cross blaze on a pine newly made.

Likewise the fourth wall mound going south, to the left of the Zagórów or Drzewce or Kalisz road — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The fifth wall mound to the right of that same road — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The sixth wall mound to the left of that same Zagórów or Kalisz road, not far from the Ląd Convent mound as a marker of the separated [pine forest] to the right of that road — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The seventh wall mound not far from the dam at the stawisko, to the right of that same road — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The eighth wall mound after crossing the filled-in ditch at the dam of that stawisko, to the right of the old Zagórów

or Kalisz road — twenty-five pręty — heaped, and that mound is surrounded with hornbeam.

The ninth wall mound to the right of that same road, on the small field or strip above the Łukomia meadows [i.e., to the north of the Łukomia meadows] — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The tenth wall mound likewise to the right of that same road, at the end of that small field or strip — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped; beside that mound is the old mound to its right, and a cross blaze worked in an oak.

The eleventh wall mound to the right of that same road — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The twelfth mound to the right of that same road, on bare ground — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The thirteenth wall mound to the left of that same road — twenty-five pręty — heaped; beside that mound is an old blaze, well prominent, cut trough-fashion on a pine.

The fourteenth wall mound to the left of that same road, in low ground — twenty-five pręty, close to the hill to the right of that road — heaped; beside that mound is also an old blaze cut on a pine.

The fifteenth wall mound to the left of that same road — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The sixteenth wall mound to the left of the old road, not far from the drainage strip stretching from the said stawisko — twenty-five pręty, and not far from the Łomowo field — heaped.

The seventeenth wall mound to the right of that same Zagórów or Kalisz road, beside the cross road from Łukomia to the Trąbczyn olędry, on bare ground above the low ground [i.e., to the north of the low ground] — heaped.

The eighteenth wall mound to the right of the old Zagórów or

Drzewce road to Kalisz, Chocz, and Tomice, as described above, beside the cross road from Łukomia and Łomowo to Królikowo — likewise twenty-five pręty, on bare ground opposite the Łomowo field lying some tens of pręty away — heaped; beside that mound is a well-prominent old mound and a cross newly cut on a pine to the left.

The nineteenth wall mound to the right of that same old road, going south, not far from the new Łomowo clearings — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The twentieth wall mound to the right of the road below the small hill, not far from those same new Łomowo clearings — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The twenty-first wall mound to the left of that same Kalisz road and to the right of the second road from Łomowo to Królikowo, beyond the drainage strip touching the Kalisz road at its end — not far, twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The twenty-second wall mound to the right of the Kalisz road beside the very Kalisz ford — likewise twenty-five pręty, close to the new Łomowo clearings — heaped.

The twenty-third wall mound to the left of the Kalisz road — that is going left southward and leaving to the right the road from Zagórów, Drzewce, Łomowo, and Łukomia past Grądzeń to Tomice running west — and running left southward along the Kalisz road, immediately after the ford — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped to the left of that road; beside that mound is one old and one new cross blaze cut on a pine, and opposite that blaze a small icon is cut into a pine.

[Translator's note: Obrazek w sosnę wcięty: "a small icon cut into a pine". Religious wayside images carved into roadside trees were a common vernacular devotional practice in early modern Poland. Translated literally; the precise form is not specified.]

The twenty-fourth mound

wall mound between the old and the new Kalisz road, beside the low ground — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The twenty-fifth wall mound likewise at the very branching of the Kalisz and old Tomice roads, the latter branching right from the beaten Kalisz road toward Tomice toward the small mill — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The twenty-sixth wall mound to the right of the old Kalisz road, going south, a little before the cross beaten road from Trąbczyn — formerly through pine and now through the Trąbczyn olędry — to Tomice, beside the Pyzdry corner mounds lying a half-mile west from the Kalisz road toward Tomice — and joining the first above-mentioned Tomice road a few staj above below the hill — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped; beside that mound is a new and an old blaze on a pine.

The twenty-seventh wall mound to the right of the old Kalisz road — twenty-five pręty, in pine — heaped.

The twenty-eighth wall mound likewise above that old Kalisz road in pine — heaped; beside that mound a cross newly made on a pine to the right of the old Kalisz road.

The twenty-ninth wall mound likewise — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The thirtieth wall mound to the right of the old Kalisz road, beyond the small ford and not far from the road from the olędry to Szymanowice crossing through the Kalisz road — between the drainage strips stretching from the duck bogs on both sides of the Kalisz road — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The thirty-first wall mound going south to the left of the Kalisz road, above the drainage strip stretching from the duck bogs [i.e., to the north of it] — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The thirty-second wall mound to the right of the road, not far from the bogs stretching from the duck bogs

— likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped; beside that mound a new cross cut on a pine.

The thirty-third wall mound to the right of the old road, on bare ground — likewise heaped.

The thirty-fourth wall mound going south above the small porowa [i.e., to the north of it] crossing through that road — beside which is the forked pine standing on the mound, with old blazes upon it well prominent — twenty-five pręty, to the left of the Kalisz road — heaped.

The thirty-fifth wall mound to the left of that same Kalisz road — likewise twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The thirty-sixth wall mound to the right of that same road, in low ground, at the same distance — heaped.

The thirty-seventh wall mound to the right of the Kalisz road, on bare ground — twenty-five pręty — heaped.

The thirty-eighth wall mound to the left of the road — beside which a cross is cut on a pine — thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirty-ninth wall mound going south toward Studzienka, to the right of the road — thirty pręty — heaped; opposite that mound, about one and a half staj west from the Kalisz road, lies the first of the eight mounds called Cygany, in low ground.

The fortieth, likewise heaped on bare ground.

The forty-first to the right of that same road — likewise thirty pręty, on bare ground — heaped.

The forty-second to the left of that same road — thirty pręty — likewise heaped.

The forty-third going south to the right of that same road — likewise thirty pręty — likewise heaped.

The forty-fourth

wall mound to the right of that same Kalisz road, in low ground — thirty pręty;

And from Studzienka and the tavern hills and bogs lying there — eighteen pręty — heaped.

All those mounds described above in order are wall mounds and divide the estates and pine forests: to the left of the Kalisz road — Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś; to the right — Łukomia or Łomowo.

And in those mounds are placed the same markers [znaki] as in the corner mounds, except for the bottle. After those mounds had been heaped, having come to Studzienka and the hills stretching crosswise through the Kalisz road and likewise the drainage strips on the far side of the hills or beyond the hills — called the tavern hills and bogs, described above in the boundary line — and having placed similar markers as in the other mounds at the places designated for mounds — he heaped three corner mounds as a corner, one from another in equal measure lying close together, emerging from their centre — dividing the three estates at the corner point itself here: Łukom or Łomowo, or the present Rozciszew folwark; Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś; and Biskupice. Two to the left of the Kalisz road — one for Trąbczyn to the left and one for Biskupice to the right of Studzienka below the hill — and the third for Łukomia to the right of the Kalisz road likewise below the hill — heaped. At those corner mounds are

[Translator's note: znaki, in Old Polish: A notch in a tree or an artificial mound to mark the boundary separating two areas belonging to two different owners, or the boundary itself. (https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/znak#Old_Polish)]

two crosses on two pines — one old and burnt, and on the other newly cut.

Likewise not far from those corner mounds on the far side of the hills — through which the Kalisz road passes between the mounds — between the bogs is the large stone, three łokci long, lying crosswise beside the very Kalisz road, not very raised above the ground, which stands as custodian of the corner mounds; beside that stone is the road branching right from the Kalisz road toward Rozciszew, running above the hill.

Having thus heaped those mounds, the said commission court and likewise the Sub-chamberlain office — that is the above-mentioned Boundary Surveyor — orders that the Honourable Prusimski, complying both with the Crown law and with the sentence of his court, shall swear on his last and final mound together with six witnesses equal to him in birth, in the formula prescribed by the statute: "As I have rightly and justly made this present demarcation by mounds and this boundary line, and have taken nothing whatsoever from the other party's ground by this present demarcation and mounds, nor have I wronged my neighbour."

For the proclamation of that oath and formula, the loyal Court Summoner Bartłomiej of Trąbczyn, assisting this present act, is assigned.

The Honourable Prusimski, complying therewith both with the Crown law and with the sentence of his court, performed that oath with six witnesses possessing their own estates — namely the Honourables Józef Pawłowski, Antoni Lipiński, Stefan Waliszewski, Jan Zalanowski, Jan Bagiński, and Piotr Makowiecki — on the image of Christ the Lord crucified, in the formula described above, on the last final (axialny) Trąbczyn mound, in the presence of many worthy persons.

[Translator's note: Acjalny / axialny: the source gives acjalny in Roman type and axialny in square brackets, indicating the transcriber's reading of what is presumably the same word — the Latin/polonised axialis, meaning "final" or "axial" (the last and decisive mound in the series upon which the oath is taken). Retained as "final" in the text; the dual form is flagged here.]

After that performance, having acknowledged the compliance with the sentence of his court, the court wishes to hold and confirms this present demarcation, marked by mounds and other signs, as inviolable; and ordered the Court Summoner to proclaim it.

[Section 7: Court's Resolution on the Trąbczyn–Biskupice Boundary — Mound-Heaping — Damages — Annulment of the Delivery of Possession — Default Judgement — Signatory Block]

After the mounds — both corner and wall mounds — had been heaped in the boundary between Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś and Łukomia or Łomowo, and after the oath had been performed by the Honourable Prusimski and his witnesses on the last Trąbczyn mound, the present commission court, having suspended for a time its deliberations concerning the delivery of possession and the proceedings and the return of the income from the olędry, and likewise concerning the claims for

transgressions and damages arising from the now demarcated boundary — proceeding to the resolution of the second boundary, between Trąbczyn or Nowa Wieś and Biskupice, or rather of the boundary line run by the Honourable Prusimski in that boundary in absentia, as ordered to be finally adjudged by the constitution or present commission even in default — having made and heard the controversies and proceeded to read the sworn inquiry, and having read it as diligently as possible, and having confronted it with the documents and with the position and layout of places subjected to the required visual inspection — since it is shown both from the documents submitted at the review of the eight mounds and blazes of the same act and year expressed above, and from the sworn inquiry subjected to the required visual inspection that although the eight mounds and forty-one blazes are lying above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of the Lusnia woodland], about one staj — and in some places more — from that woodland, and stretching one and a half staj from the Kalisz road toward the corduroy crossings and Czarny Bród to the east — they are actually present and shown at

the review; and those mounds and blazes — both from the documents, namely the judicial site inspection of 1644 in the Pyzdry municipal court on Saturday before the Feast of the Holy Trinity by the Trąbczyn heirs against the Biskupice heirs together with the protest concerning the heaping of those mounds and blazes, and the default decree and two decrees from controversies with motions to the Crown Tribunal of the same year, and likewise two Piotrków Tribunal decrees of deferral in 1645, and the third Tribunal decree of 1646 concerning the heaping of those mounds and blazes against the Biskupice heirs, and finally the Konin land court decree of 1647 from the Tribunal decree and the oath performed thereat by the Honourable Biskupski — all described more fully in the review — and likewise from the present sworn inquiries: are shown to have been heaped privately and by night on proper Trąbczyn ground, at great expense and detriment to the Trąbczyn heirs, above the Lusnia woodland [i.e., to the north of it] beginning from the corduroy crossings; and for that reason they are called Cygany.

And so those mounds and blazes themselves show the further continuity and breadth of the Trąbczyn ground — taking it from the Kalisz road and past those mounds and blazes toward the corduroy crossings, as shown from the sworn inquiry not far from Czarny Bród, from which the blazes and mounds began to be heaped there toward the corduroy crossings; and further toward the Biskupice corner mound with Trąbczyn and Łazy — lying one staj from Czarny Bród beside the beaten road from Szetlewek to Jarocin — and to that same corner mound and to Czarny Bród it is shown from the sworn inquiry to reach. And as to the other side — the breadth of that Trąbczyn ground to the south of those mounds — although the Honourable Prusimski does not prove in his court by any documents to which estate it extends and with which estate it meets there — nor the judicial site inspection upon those mounds and blazes ordered by the Tribunal decrees, from which the last Tribunal decree of 1646, ordering the performance of the enjoined oath, issued; nor the second judicial site inspection of

1655 by Głoskowski, Kalisz Boundary Surveyor, expressed in the two Bydgoszcz Tribunal decrees — nor any proceeding or field decree nor earlier summons — yet from the sworn inquiries alone, not only the present boundary line but still further — past and beyond the boundary line — toward Otnie and Gąsiennice or Myszynice, as some witnesses testified and attested the further use of the ground beyond Studzienka, it is apparent.

And moreover that the heir of Biskupice, Biskupski — although it is not definitively established that he was to stand at Studzienka — yet was to stand at the Kalisz road and establish his boundaries there; and that the Biskupice ground or his convergence is to be found here at Studzienka — is shown from the sworn inquiry. And moreover that the large mound above [i.e., to the north of] the road from Szetlewek through Czarny Bród to Jarocin, lying about one staj before Czarny Bród, beside which are two pines with crosses and a third felled likewise with a cross, is an actual corner mound — one for three — and there the estates of Łazy, Trąbczyn Wielki or the deserted lands Drzęzgi, and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce converge.

And there in that place and further to Czarny Bród from that corner mound they hold the Szetlew road as a boundary and peacefully use the ground and pine forest — Trąbczyn and Biskupice each on their side. Therefore the present commission court, weighing those proofs, adjudges, assigns, approves, confirms, and ratifies the full and entire boundary line of the said Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice — both from the starting point and through and to the terminal point — that is to the above-mentioned corner mound lying one staj before Czarny Bród — and likewise the boundary line in the third boundary from that corner mound between Trąbczyn Wielki or the deserted lands Drzęzgi and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce — from the corner mound to Czarny Bród, run for one staj, unable to be determined on account of excessively high water — as legitimately run to that point — to the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice. And for the greater security thereof, pursuant to the statute's disposition, orders him to swear on the last corner mound described and heaped above, with six witnesses of equal birth, in the formula prescribed by the statute — after the mounds have been heaped. And returning to the corner mound established at Studzienka in the boundary of Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś, it proceeds to heap mounds together with the above-mentioned Honourable Wojciech Czarnecki, Kalisz Boundary Surveyor.

And beginning that heaping of mounds toward the east — the said Boundary Surveyor, having measured twelve eight-łokieć pręty from the corner mounds and Studzienka to the boggy marsh and having placed the same markers as in the other mounds at the designated mound place —

[Translator's note: The commission's definition of the pręt: The source at this point reads dwanaście prętów ośm łokciowych — "twelve pręty of eight łokci" — where ośm łokciowych directly qualifies prętów, defining each pręt used in this survey as an eight-łokieć unit. This is confirmed by an earlier passage (the review of the Drzewce–Trąbczyn wall mounds, f. 680v), where inter-mound distances are stated in łokci and the phrase jako się z wymiaru laską osiem łokciową pokazuje — "as shown by measurement with an eight-łokieć staff" — makes explicit that the commission's measuring instrument throughout was an eight-łokieć staff, one length of which constitutes one pręt in this record. The standard Polish pręt of the period was 15 łokci; the commission's 8-łokieć pręt is therefore a local working unit specific to this survey. Using the standard 18th-century Polish łokieć of approximately 59.5 cm, one commission pręt ≈ 4.76 m. The most frequent mound interval in the record (25 pręty) thus corresponds to approximately 119 m; 30 pręty ≈ 143 m; 35 pręty ≈ 167 m.]

Heaped the first mound, lying east from the above-described corner mound before the very bog at the beginning of that boundary of Trąbczyn Mały or Nowa Wieś with Biskupice below the hill.

And from that mound, going further along that same boggy and boundary-line-cut marsh straight east, the second mound is heaped — thirty pręty — in which three stakes one łokieć above the mound are driven, and boundary markers — stones and brick only — are placed.

Likewise going further — the third mound in that same bog — thirty pręty — and three stakes in it;

The fourth mound and three stakes in it in that same bog — likewise with stakes — heaped.

The fifth mound, coming out a little onto drier though still low ground — likewise thirty pręty, without stakes — heaped.

The sixth mound above the boggy ground — thirty pręty — heaped.

The seventh mound and three stakes in it, on bog — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The eighth, ninth, tenth, eleventh, twelfth, thirteenth, fourteenth, fifteenth,

sixteenth, seventeenth mounds in a straight line going east, on bare ground — each thirty pręty — heaped.

The eighteenth mound on bare ground below the hill — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The nineteenth mound on bare ground on a small hill — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The twentieth mound — likewise thirty pręty — not far from the road which runs from Szetlewek to Szymanowice to Jarocin — heaped.

The twenty-first mound to the left of the Szetlew or Jarocin road, beside the cross road which runs from Łomowo to Królikowo — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The twenty-second and twenty-third mounds to the right of that same Szetlew road — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The twenty-fourth mound to the left of the road — at equal distance — heaped.

The twenty-fifth wall mound to the left of the Szetlew or Jarocin road, beside the cross road which runs from Łazy past the Trąbczyn olędry through the Czarniawa drainage strip and across the road from Łomowo to Królikowo — thirty-five pręty — heaped.

The twenty-sixth mound to the right of that same Szetlew road — thirty pręty — heaped.

The twenty-seventh mound to the left of that same road, close to the Czarniawa drainage strip

— likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The twenty-eighth mound to the right of that same road, above the very Czarniawa drainage strip [i.e., to the north of the drainage strip] — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The twenty-ninth mound to the right of that same road, above the drainage strip [i.e., to the north of it], beside the second road which runs from the Trąbczyn olędry to Królikowo — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirtieth wall mound to the right of the Szetlew road, above the drainage strip [i.e., to the north of it] — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirty-first mound to the right of the road.

The thirty-second mound to the left of the road, above the Czarniawa drainage strip [i.e., to the north of it] — heaped in equal measure.

The thirty-third mound to the left of that same Szetlew road, going east as all the others from the beginning, above that same drainage strip [i.e., to the north of it] — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirty-fourth mound to the right of that same road — thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirty-fifth mound to the left.

The thirty-sixth and thirty-seventh mounds to the right of the road — likewise thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirty-eighth mound to the left of that same road — thirty pręty — heaped.

The thirty-ninth corner mound — a large one, one for three — beside that same road from Szetlewek

Mały to Jarocin, to the left of the road, straight east, one staj from Czarny Bród — heaped; which marks the confluence or ground and pine forest of three estates: Łazy, Trąbczyn Wielki or [the deserted lands] Drzęzgi, and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce. Beside that newly heaped mound to the left is a similar old mound called Tomal, left intact; beside that newly heaped mound are three crosses newly cut on three pines, and two old ones — and beside the old mound stands a pine with a cross, felled. In all those mounds, apart from those heaped on the bogs with stakes, the following markers are placed: brick, slag, glass, and charcoal.

And all those mounds, from Studzienka and the tavern hills and bogs — or rather from the corner mound established there — all the way to that large corner mound just described called Tomal, in one and the same easterly direction — heaped. After those mounds had been heaped in such manner, the present commission court and likewise the Sub-chamberlain office orders that the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, heir of Trąbczyn Wielki, Łazy, Osiny, and Nowa Wieś or

Trąbczyn — complying both with the Crown law and with the sentence of his court in the performance of the oath — shall swear this boundary line as run and assigned to him in this boundary as well between the above-mentioned estates — on the last same corner mound just heaped, together with six witnesses equal to him in birth, in the formula prescribed by the statute: "As I have rightly and justly made this present demarcation by mounds and this boundary line, and have taken nothing whatsoever from the other party's ground by this present demarcation and mounds, nor have I wronged my neighbour." For the proclamation of that oath and formula the loyal Court Summoner Bartłomiej of Trąbczyn, assisting this present act, is assigned.

The Honourable Prusimski, complying therewith by the order of his court and with the Crown law, performed that oath with six witnesses possessing their own estates: namely the Honourables and Honest Kasper, father, and Benedykt, his son, Kurowski, heirs of a portion of Kurowo; Józef Pawłowski; Jan Bagiński; Stefan Waliszewski; Piotr Makowiecki — on the image of Christ the Lord crucified, in the formula described above, on the said

last corner mound newly heaped beside the old Tomal, in the presence of many worthy and distinguished persons, together with the witnesses.

After that compliant performance, the present court, having acknowledged the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice's compliance in this case, wishes to hold and confirms this present demarcation, marked by mounds and other signs, as inviolable; and ordered the Court Summoner to proclaim it.

After the demarcation of that boundary — since the Honourable Prusimski, in the boundary begun from the said corner mound between Trąbczyn Wielki or the deserted lands Drzęzgi and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce, unable to be concluded due to excessive flooding, likewise has — as to that boundary also — the boundary line from the said corner mound as far as Czarny Bród assigned and adjudged to him.

The commission court therefore orders the said Kalisz Boundary Surveyor also in that boundary — beside the very Czarny Bród and the beaten Szetlew road to Jarocin — to stand guard over of the corner mound, to heap [a mound] in the presence of his court — without prejudice to the further continuation and resolution from this point of that boundary — at the future appointed date of the adjournment, and to that end adjourns its further act for

the resolution and conclusion of that boundary to the twenty-first day of May of the coming year 1776; and with all that contained therein, limits and prorogues the commission from the Commonwealth, with all the powers granted to it.

And thereupon, having proceeded to confront with the boundary lines and position of places the map delineated by the Most Illustrious Father Surveyor during the present commission and submitted to its court at the determination of the commission with his signature — having confronted it with the position and layout of places accurately and found it conforming and agreeing in all — and signed by the Most Honourable President with the consent of his whole court — it issues [the map] to the Honourable Prusimski. And nonetheless the present court orders the printed schedule of documents produced in its court — signed by the President — to be either inserted alongside the present decree or submitted to the acta for oblata.

After the conclusion of the demarcation of the above-mentioned estates and their boundaries, the present commission court — resolving its suspension concerning the transgressions committed with the Honourable Chełmski expressed above — first finds a sworn inquiry to be necessary, ordering the Honourable Prusimski to draw up the interrogatories in that matter, sign them, and submit them to his court for the hearing of witnesses; and to introduce into it witnesses not involved in this matter, credible, and — having first sworn the preceding oath of non-subornation — trustworthy. The Honourable Prusimski, complying with his court's sentence, drew up, signed, and submitted the interrogatories to his court.

And thereupon performed the oath in the customary formula of non-subornation of those witnesses in any improper manner — before his court, on the image of the Passion of Christ the Lord — before the introduction of the witnesses for the sworn inquiry. The court, having acknowledged his compliance with its sentence, proceeded to hear the sworn inquiry — and having first heard from each witness individually the oath for truthful testimony, conducted and heard those sworn inquiries in the customary manner. After hearing them, and having heard the further controversies of the parties introduced into its court — it proceeded to the reading of those sworn inquiries, both those just conducted by it, and the second conducted in 1728 by the Kalisz land court authority concerning the transgressions also then attempted. And having thoroughly informed itself from both those and from the boundary documents, and having read them as diligently as possible — as to the transgressions and the claims arising from the now demarcated ground — since it is shown both from the sworn inquiries and from the judicial site inspections, manifests, and protests repeatedly suspended and made by the predecessors of the Honourable Starost of Niszczewice: that the Honourable Chełmski and likewise his father — having raised the controversy over the stawisko or rather the empty marshes and the other pine forests and woodlands now demarcated

as proper Trąbczyn property — frequently sent their people violently onto those pine forests, and especially the Lusnia, Miąsykąt, and the pine beside them, and likewise onto the stawisko, and for several dozen years had not a little timber felled — partly to the glassworks, partly for other building needs, and at times gave it to others. And likewise onto the stawisko or rather empty marshes — belonging to Trąbczyn pursuant to the partitions of 1522 and 1693 and the old boundaries, now demarcated — they frequently and violently intruded, and having several times there made hay took it away, and forbade the people of Nowa Wieś and Osiny to use them; and frequently and violently ordered a weir or rather a dam — on another's ground, in the manner of a small weir — to be made on that stawisko, several times when it was broken by Trąbczyn; and by that dam made by force, and by the water held through it and through their own [dam], they frequently flooded not only that ground which the Honourable Chełmski was now taking in his boundary line, but also that which remained outside his boundary line, and likewise the meadows, pastures, and pine forests growing upon the empty stawisko as well as lying above it; and by that flooding dried out not a little timber, and caused significant damage in both the meadows and the pastures and the pine forests and woodlands — of which still not a little dry timber, especially on the stawisko, both fallen and standing, is present and found. And likewise committed other transgressions against the heirs and people of Trąbczyn. And likewise from twelve olędry, for four years the Honourable Chełmski collected annual cash rents,

specified item by item from each separately in the register submitted by the Honourable Prusimski to his court with his own signature and signed by the commission President — and used them in various ways and for various services; and with the right of propination at the inn on the highway, for those same four years took no small income from spirits and beer.

And moreover it is shown from the sworn inquiries now conducted that the Honourable Chełmski — having received the olędry and the inn through the delivery of possession — and moreover as will be expressed below, contrary to the statute law on boundaries and by an unlawful proceeding — when the Honourable Prusimski, obstructing at least that unlawful possession of the inn, had settled his own people in that inn, the Honourable Chełmski cried out to beat, kill, and shoot [them]; and likewise ordered all the items in the inn, expressed in the register submitted to his court by the Honourable Starost of Niszczewice with his own signature and signed by the commission President, to be seized — and did not surrender them to the [municipal court] chancery.

Therefore the present commission court, weighing these unlawful acts as condemned by the Crown law and disturbing the peace of the nobility, and manifesting the insufficient regard for the Crown law — deferring to those Crown laws prohibiting also the forcible seizure and appropriation of one's own property greatly compounded in controversy of ground —

and moreover preventing similar excesses from being penalised: the Honourable Stanisław Chełmski, for those transgressions committed and attached to his own person and the person of the same — arising both from the ground transgression and from the violent attack and shooting and the burning of the inn and for the unlawful use of firearms [pro abusu bombardarum] — the personal penalties being set aside as to the person of his father, regularly owed though they are, extinguished with his death —

it finds and orders that the said Honourable Chełmski shall sit in the tower of the Konin municipal court for twelve weeks from the time of the present decree, and shall continue that sitting for two weeks consecutively; and upon leaving the tower shall pay and count out — four starost's pledges also in respect of the offence committed by his father, and separately for the unlawful use of firearms three hundred grzywien — to the Honourable Prusimski or his authorised plenipotentiary in the Konin municipal court, under outlawry in case of non-compliance with that decree, to be followed by the manifest there lodged at that same court.

As to the damages caused by the flooding — of pine forests felled both to the glassworks and for buildings, and for fuel; and from the meadows partaken for a substantial time; and the damages caused to the people thereby; and likewise for the services had from the olędry — the present commission court finds it just that all this is owed from the said Honourable Chełmski, heir of Łukomia, to the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, heir of Trąbczyn.

And although a far larger sum would be owed to the Honourable Prusimski for those claims, nonetheless the court, fixing no larger sum than eight thousand Polish złoty for the Honourable Prusimski by the moderation of its court —

and ordering the Honourable Starost of Niszczewice to swear that moderated sum without delay before his court, on the image of Christ the Lord crucified, in this formula: "that that sum is justly owed to him, as moderated by his court, for the felled pine forests and woodlands, and the drying out of various timber, and for other wrongs done to him" — and assigns to him the Court Summoner for the proclamation of that oath and formula.

The Honourable Prusimski swore in the presence of his court and many other persons. To that oath the present commission court orders that the said Honourable Chełmski shall pay and count out that sum, sworn by the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, within eight weeks from the time and act of the present decree in the present year — under outlawry before that same authority and the Konin court records, with the manifest to be lodged there in case of non-compliance at that same appointed date — to the said Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, or his authorised plenipotentiary; [along with the] lawful penalty of fourteen grzywien to that same party, payable likewise at that same appointed date under similar outlawry.

As to the rent from the twelve olędry received in 1771 through the delivery of possession made in the Lusnia woodland on Monday after the Feast of All Saints and filed in the Konin municipal court on Monday after Saint Andrew the Apostle of the same year — since it is shown both from the sworn inquiry and from that same delivery of possession that the Honourable Chełmski received twelve olędry and the inn with stabling; and those olędry from the settler's rent — that is from the fields and meadows pursuant to law and statute — paid annual rent to the Honourable Starost of Niszczewice, their lord, of

six hundred and seventy Polish złoty and twenty-six grosze;

and that rent the Honourable Chełmski collected from them annually and punctually, and reckoning four years from the time of the delivery of possession to the present act — collected for four years the sum of two thousand six hundred and eighty-eight Polish złoty and fourteen grosze —

the present commission court, combining likewise the second sum of three thousand four hundred and eighty-six Polish złoty — reckoning only the profit from beer and spirits for propination, and likewise for the items seized in the inn during the violent attack and its seizure, paid to the noblemen — specified item by item in the register submitted by the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, to his court with his own signature and signed by the President of the present commission —

both sums into one total sum of six thousand one hundred and sixty-nine Polish złoty and fourteen grosze — finds and adjudges it to be justly owed to the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, from the Honourable Chełmski, by way of satisfaction, upon the hearing first of the oath therefor. And ordering the said Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, to swear that sum in the following formula: "as that sum is justly owed to him, as adjudged, for the seized rent from the olędry and the income from the inn, and for the items seized in that inn during the violent expulsion of the Honourable Prusimski" — without delay before his court, on the image of Christ the Lord crucified — assigns to him the Court Summoner. The Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, swore and likewise swore that sum.

After that oath the commission court, having found that sum to be genuine and just, orders that the Honourable Chełmski shall pay and count it out to the Honourable Prusimski or his authorised plenipotentiary in the present year

at the above-mentioned appointed date — together with the above sum — before that same authority and the Konin municipal court records, genuinely and punctually; under similar outlawry in case of non-compliance with the present decree, with the manifest lodged together for both sums at that same appointed date at that authority; [along with the] lawful penalty of fourteen grzywien payable to the said Honourable Prusimski at that same appointed date and place under similar outlawry.

As to the lawfulness of the delivery of possession and the proceeding — although the first default judgement of 1761 on Monday after Saint Bartholomew the Apostle, subsequently [obtained] on the late Honourable Antoni Prusimski, uncle of the present plaintiff — after the filed court appearance and withdrawal from it, with the hand-pledge adjournment made by him with the Honourables Chełmski brothers on the twenty-second April in Trąbczyn of that same year 1761, past the nephew who is the present plaintiff — was obtained at the instance of both Honourable Chełmski brothers, and is shown to have been filed in the Konin municipal court on Wednesday after Saint Matthew the Apostle of the same year. Yet since that default judgement was obtained after the decree had issued — contrary to the statute law on boundary causes, which orders all co-heirs of any estate whatsoever, not excepting also minors, to belong to the boundary cause and to respond in it — on the foundation of which law the late Honourable Antoni Prusimski, uncle of the present plaintiff, having — past his nephew who had never been in his guardianship, and moreover who at that time already had full age and was managing his own affairs — filed a manifest in the Konin municipal court on Wednesday after

Saint Bartholomew the Apostle of that same year 1761 — concerning the invalidity of the adjournment, and also that he cannot respond and be adjudged without his nephew and co-heir of Trąbczyn — defended himself with that manifest; and accordingly on the foundation of that manifest and likewise of the terminus attested by himself and his nephew in the Konin municipal court in the same year — sought referral to the Tribunal. And the field court, not heeding those defences and the law — past the nephew and without his court appearance — making him as though guardian of that nephew, ordered him alone to dispute with the Honourables and Honest Chełmski in the present cause, and by that unlawful sentence compelled him to withdraw his court appearance — and in such an unlawful manner was it obtained. And thereupon the present plaintiff, the Honourable Prusimski, proves and demonstrates to his court that he was never in that uncle's guardianship; and moreover proves by lease contracts concluded with the late Honourable Bińkowski and likewise by his birth certificate that he already at that time had full age and was managing his own affairs. Therefore the present commission court — weighing both the Crown statute law on boundaries and the above-mentioned proofs — finds the unlawful sentence of the above-mentioned field court and likewise that default judgement unlawfully obtained and issued against the late Honourable Antoni Prusimski, uncle of the present plaintiff — overturns it and quashes it without penalty. And for the same reasons and grounds releases the Honourable Starost of Niszczewice, nephew as successor of the same, from the penalty for contravention — insofar as it would fall half upon the person of the late Honourable Antoni Prusimski, uncle.

As to the other half of that contravention of the Tribunal decree — or rather the penalty therefor — since the present plaintiff, the Honourable Prusimski,

the nephew, did not register at the court appearance appointed date and thereby contravened the Tribunal decree — the present commission court, imposing upon him the half penalty for that contravention, adjudges one starost's pledge owed to the Honourable Chełmski from him.

As to the second default judgement — obtained in the Poznań Tribunal on Thursday after the Exaltation of the Holy Cross of 1767 against the present plaintiff, after the filed court appearance, at the instance of the Honourables Chełmski — since that was overturned by the Bydgoszcz Tribunal decree of Monday after the Feast of the Visitation of the Blessed Virgin Mary of 1768, and a half-double pledge was adjudged to the Honourable Chełmski for it — the court therefore adjudges that same half-double pledge owed to the Honourable Chełmski from the said Honourable Prusimski.

As to the contravention of the Tribunal decree of Tuesday on the day after the Feast of Saint Aegidius of 1766 — since the Honourable Prusimski acted against that decree by appealing it, and that decree was improved by the later Bydgoszcz Tribunal decree expressed above; nor did the Honourable Chełmski himself bring it to effect; and moreover since the later decree, resolving the default judgement expressed above, did not adjudge and resolve that contravention of the said decree — therefore the present commission court does not recognise that contravention and releases the present plaintiff, the Honourable Prusimski, from it as free.

As to the contravention of the last Bydgoszcz Tribunal decree

of 1768 expressed above of the present act, and likewise the last default judgement therefrom — rather from the adjournment — filed in the Kalisz municipal court on Monday after the Feast of the Eleven Thousand Virgins of 1770 — the present commission court, resolving that contravention and default judgement, orders that the Honourable Prusimski, Starost of Niszczewice, shall pay and count out to the Honourable Chełmski for that contravention of the said last decree and likewise for the default judgement obtained on the foundation of that decree — three starost's pledges together with the above-mentioned one and a half pledge adjudged by his court — at the appointed date for receiving the sums and grzywien adjudged to him from the Honourable Chełmski as above, before the Konin municipal court records and authority — to the said Honourable Chełmski, under outlawry upon the manifest lodged there in case of non-compliance with the present decree; or to deduct them from the sums and grzywien adjudged to the Honourable Chełmski.

As to the delivery of possession of the olędry and the grounds in controversy — made in the Lusnia woodland in 1771 on Monday after the Feast of All Saints by the Honourable Wawrzyniec Wstowski, Commissioner of the Poznań and Kalisz voivodeships, on behalf and in favour of the Honourable Stanisław Chełmski, heir of the Łukomia estate — filed in the Konin municipal court on Monday after Saint Andrew the Apostle of the same year.

Since that delivery of possession is shown not only to have been conducted by a bad proceeding resolved above by his court — and entirely only upon two default judgements — but also contrary to the statute law on

boundary causes; which law in boundary causes orders not delivery of possession upon a completed proceeding but the serving of citations from the Sub-chamberlain in the prescribed order for the absentee demarcation — and contrary to that law and custom; and moreover without the writ of execution established by new law — is shown to his court to be unlawful: not so much carried out officially as rather violently; and moreover conducted on another's ground and with another's people now demarcated by his court.

Therefore the court of the present commission, finding that delivery of possession to be unlawful, overturns, quashes, and annihilates it. And thereupon orders those olędry and the inn with stabling, and the grounds and pine forests surrendered to the Honourable Chełmski through that delivery of possession — insofar as they are now already demarcated and marked by mounds — to be immediately vacated and returned on behalf and in favour of the Honourable Prusimski. And rather, the court itself immediately places the Honourable Prusimski in possession — under outlawry in case of opposition to its court's judgements and obstruction of the Honourable Prusimski's possession within eight weeks from the time of the decree, to be determined at the Konin municipal court upon the manifest lodged there. And since the Honourable Chełmski and likewise the Honourable Wstowski, Commissioner of the Poznań and Kalisz voivodeships, unheard-of and not practised in a boundary cause — conducted such a delivery of possession in a woodland and rather violently than officially; and thereby transgressed and

violated the law both on boundaries and on writs of execution — the court therefore holds the Honourable Chełmski to be subject to penalty — both for himself and for his official. And although a larger penalty would be due pursuant to the new law, yet the court, reducing it and omitting the personal penalty, orders that the Honourable Chełmski — both for himself and standing for his official — shall pay to the Honourable Prusimski or his authorised plenipotentiary one thousand grzywien pursuant to the new law on boundaries — likewise within eight weeks, in the Konin municipal court in the present year — under outlawry at that same appointed date, upon a separate manifest lodged.

As to the pretended expulsion and penalty claimed by the Honourable Chełmski against the Honourable Prusimski — since that delivery of possession, as expressed above, was unlawfully conducted, and the Honourable Prusimski, defending his own property, only settled his own people in the inn — whom the Honourable Chełmski, as expressed above, was evident from the sworn inquiry, expelled — the court therefore, deferring to justice, releases from all penalty claimed by the Honourable Chełmski — both the Honourable Prusimski and his people.

As to the contravention of the Piotrków Tribunal decree of Thursday after Saint Martin of 1760 in this cause, and the default judgement after withdrawal from the court appearance — from that same field decree, obtained in the field on Monday after the Sunday Exaudi of the same year at the instance of the Honourable Prusimski against the Honourable Chełmski, filed in the Konin municipal court on Friday on the very feast of

Saint Anthony of Padua — since it is shown from that very field act expressed above that the Honourable Chełmski together with his brother, not having proved their convergence or corner mound, and indeed — having overturned the table on which the official was writing, as expressed in the field act — withdrew from the court appearance and submitted themselves as beaten;

and thereby contravened both the Tribunal decree and the field decree — the present commission court therefore orders that the Honourable Chełmski, discharging that default judgement, shall pay half-double pledge, and for the contravention the double pledge pursuant to law — at the above-mentioned appointed date at the Konin municipal court — to the Honourable Prusimski, together with the grzywien adjudged above, under similar outlawry in case of contravention.

As to the costs and expenses claimed by the Honourable Prusimski to be satisfied by the Honourable Chełmski — the present commission court, suspending its resolution of that claimed costs category, reserves it for adjudication at the above-mentioned appointed date arising from the adjournment in the boundary between Trąbczyn Wielki or Drzęzgi and Biskupice or the deserted lands of Bystrzyce. And since this act proceeds in a split court — the court, having sealed four sworn inquiries: one of 1728 concerning the stawisko, a second of 1760 concerning the corner mound, a third concerning the entire

boundary line of the Honourable Prusimski, and a fourth concerning the transgressions and claims, all conducted in the present year by its court — delivers and issues those sworn inquiries to the Honourable Prusimski, and likewise the map confronted and signed by the President.

Whereof this present decree gives satisfaction, by the force of the law or constitution enacted by the Estates of the Commonwealth.

Ludwik Dąmbski, Voivode of Brest Kuyavia, President of the commission, (signed in own hand) [place of the family seal]

[uncertain: His Worship] Brzeziński, Starost of Inowłocław, commissioner, (signed in own hand) [place of the family seal]

Stanisław Dąmbski, Standard-bearer of Brest Kuyavia, commissioner holding the scribal pen, (signed in own hand) [place of the family seal]

Jan Kwilecki of Kwilcz, Cup-bearer of Wschów, commissioner, (signed in own hand) [place of the family seal]

Kajetan Umiński, Cup-bearer of Kowal, Deputy Voivode of Brest Kuyavia, commissioner, (signed in own hand) [place of the family seal]

Kazimierz Gurczyński, commissioner, (signed in own hand) [place of the family seal]

Wojciech Czarnecki, Kalisz Boundary Surveyor, heaping mounds in their presence pursuant to the decree of the Most Honourable and Right Honourable commissioners signed above and pursuant to that same decree, (signed in own hand).

Which, having been submitted thus, the same submitting party here at the present records left, and it is sewn in.

Extracted from the Records of the Starost's Municipal Court of Brest Kuyavia.

Zboiński (signed in own hand)

[Seal of the court]

Read (aloud) by Ubysz
